Homological theory of self-orthogonal modules


Abstract
Tachikawa’s second conjecture predicts that a finitely generated, self-orthogonal module over a finite-dimensional self-injective algebra is projective. This conjecture is an important part of the Nakayama conjecture. Our principal motivation of this work is a systematic understanding of finitely generated, self-orthogonal generators over a self-injective Artin algebra from the view point of stable module categories. Consequently, we give equivalent characterizations of Tachikawa’s second conjecture in terms of M M -Gorenstein categories, and establish a recollement of the M M -relative stable categories for a self-orthogonal generator M M . Further, we show that the Nakayama conjecture holds true for Gorenstein-Morita algebras.



Hongxing Chen
and
Changchang Xi
∗

†
†

∗
Corresponding author.
Email: xicc@cnu.edu.cn; Fax: 0086 10 68903637.
†
†

Classification: Primary 16E35, 18G80, 18G65; Secondary 16G10, 16E65, 16G50.
†

Keywords: Gorenstein-Morita algebra; Morita algebra; Recollement; Self-injective algebra; Self-orthogonal module; Stable category; Tachikawa conjecture.

1
Introduction

Since about half a century homological conjectures form a core set of problems in
representation theory and homological algebra of finite-dimensional algebras. One of the most
prominent conjectures in this system of closely related conjectures is the Nakayama conjecture posed by Nakayama in
[
32
]
.

(NC)
If a finite-dimensional algebra over a field
has infinite dominant dimension, then it is self-injective.

This conjecture has been verified
for a few classes of algebras, for which the conjecture could be checked more or less directly by
clever computations. Despite many efforts, taking various approaches, very little is known about the
homological conjectures and in particular about Nakayama’s conjecture.

To deal with the conjecture, Tachikawa proposed another two homological conjectures (see
[
40
]
), they are now called Tachikawa’s first and second conjecture.

(TC1)
If a finite-dimensional algebra
A
over a field
k
satisfies
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{A}(D(A),A)=0
for all
n\geq 1
, then
A
is self-injective, where
D={\rm Hom}_{k}(-,k)
is the usual duality.

(TC2)
Let
A
be a finite-dimensional self-injective algebra and
M
a finitely generated
A
-module. If
M
is self-orthogonal, that is,
{\rm Ext}_{A}^{n}(M,M)=0
for all
n\geq 1
, then
M
is projective.

Concerning (TC1) and (TC2), there are only a few cases verified. For instance, (TC1) holds for special local algebras and commutative algebras (see
[
2
]
and
[
4
,
22
]
, respectively), while (TC2) holds true for self-injective algebras of finite representation type, symmetric algebras with radical cube zero, and local self-injective algebras with radical cube zero (see
[
40
,
23
,
39
]
, respectively ). For further information on these conjectures, we refer to
[
44
]
.

The validity of both (TC1) and (TC2) is equivalent to the one of (NC). Moreover, by a result of Mueller
[
31
]
, a pair
(A,M)
, with
A
a self-injective algebra and
M
a finitely generated
A
-module, satisfies (TC2) if and only if the endomorphism algebra
{\rm End}_{A}(A\oplus M)
satisfies (NC).

Thus it is of significant interest to understand self-orthogonal modules of the form
A\oplus M
for a self-injective algebra
A
and finitely generated
A
-modules
M
. Generally, a self-orthogonal module of the form
B\oplus Y
over an Artin algebra
B
with
Y
a finitely generated
B
-module is termed
self-orthogonal generators
.

In this paper, we investigate self-orthogonal generators over self-injective algebras from the point of view of stable categories. More precisely, we first establish a general theory for arbitrary (not necessarily self-orthogonal) generator by constructing two pairs of triangle endofunctors for stable module categories, and then establish specially a recollement of the relative stable categories for a self-orthogonal generator. Finally, we describe compact objects of the right term of the recollement by the heart of a torsion pair in the stable module category. Based on these investigations, we give equivalent characterizations of (TC2) and show that the Nakayama conjecture holds for Gorenstein-Morita algebras.

1.1
Equivalent characterizations of Tachikawa’s second conjecture

In this section, we present our equivalent characterizations of (TC2) in terms of perpendicular categories or special modules associated with self-orthogonal generators. We then introduce the notion of Gorenstein-Morita algebras and state one of our main results, namely (NC) holds for Gorenstein-Morita algebras.

We begin with recalling a few notation and terminology.

Let
A
be an Artin algebra. We denote by
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
(respectively,
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
) the category of (respectively, finitely generated) left
A
-modules, by
D
the usual duality on
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
and by
\nu_{A}
the Nakayama functor
{{}_{A}}D(A)\otimes_{A}-
. For
M\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, let
{\rm Add}(M)
(respectively,
{\rm add}(M)
) be the full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
consisting of direct summands of (respectively, finite) direct sums of
copies of
M
, and let
M^{\bot 1}
be the full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
consisting of modules
X
with
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{A}(M,X)=0
. We say that
M
is
self-orthogonal
if
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{A}(M,M)=0
for all
i\geq 1
;
Nakayama-stable
if
{\rm add}(M)={\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))
; and a
generator
if

A\in{\rm add}(M)
. Clearly,
{}_{A}A
is Nakayama-stable if
A
is a self-injective algebra. Every module over a symmetric algebra
A
(that is,
A\simeq D(A)
as
A
-
A
-bimodules) is Nakayama-stable.

Let
A
be a self-injective algebra,
M\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
a self-orthogonal generator and
\Lambda:={\rm End}_{A}(M)
. Our strategy is to understand the relation between the category of Gorenstein-projective
\Lambda
-modules and the category of two-sided perpendicular category
\mathscr{G}
of
M
, where

\mathscr{G}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\mid{\rm Ext}_{A}^{n}(M,X)=0={\rm Ext}_{A}^{n}(X,M)\;\;\mbox{for all}\;\;n\geq 1\}

has
A
-modules left and right orthogonal to
{{}_{A}}M
. These two categories are all Frobenius and equivalent (see Lemma
3.17
). This builds a new bridge between (TC2) and (NC).
Following
[
38
]
,
\mathscr{G}
is called an
M
-Gorenstein subcategory
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.
Then the quotient category of
\mathscr{G}
modulo
{\rm Add}({{}_{A}}M)
, denoted by

\mathscr{C}:=\mathscr{G}/[M],

is a triangulated category and equivalent to the stable category of Gorenstein-projective
\Lambda
-modules.
The category
\mathscr{C}
is called an
M
-Gorenstein stable category
.
In particular, if
M=A
, then
\mathscr{C}
is the usual
stable module category
of
A
, denoted by

A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

Next, we introduce two classes of
A
-modules determined by
M
.

Definition 1.1
.

Let
X
be an
A
-module.

(i)

X
is
M
-compact
if it is a compact object in the category
\mathscr{C}
, that is,

X\in\mathscr{G}
and the functor
{\rm Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(X,-):\mathscr{C}\to\mathbb{Z}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
commutes with coproducts.

(ii)

X
is
M
-filtered
if it has a filtration

0=X_{0}\subseteq X_{1}\subseteq X_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq X_{n}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq X

in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
such that
X=\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}X_{n}
and the subquotient
X_{n+1}/X_{n}
of
X
is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of
A
-modules in the set
\{_{A}A\}\cup\{\Omega_{A}^{-i}(M)\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. If
X=X_{n}
for an integer
n
in the filtration, then
X
is said to be
finitely
M
-filtered
.

Finitely generated modules in
\mathscr{G}
are
M
-compact, and finitely
M
-filtered
A
-modules are exactly finitely generated,
M
-filtered
A
-modules. Further,
M
-compact, finitely
M
-filtered
A
-modules lie in
{\rm add}(M)
by Lemma
4.16
. Clearly,
A
-compact modules are exactly
A
-modules that are isomorphic in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
to finitely generated modules.

Now, our characterizations of (TC2) for Nakayama-stable generators read as follows.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
A
be a self-injective Artin algebra and
M
a self-orthogonal and Nakayama-stable generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. The following are equivalent.

(1)

M
is a projective
A
-module.

(2)

\mathscr{G}
coincides with the full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
consisting of all filtered colimits of finitely generated modules in
\mathscr{G}
.

(3)
Any
M
-compact and
M
-filtered
A
-module lies in
{\rm Add}(M)
.

(4)
The minimal left
\mathscr{G}
-approximation
W
of
\Omega_{A}^{-}(M)
is a filtered colimit of finitely generated modules in
\mathscr{G}
.

(5)
The minimal left
\mathscr{G}
-approximation
W
of
\Omega_{A}^{-}(M)
has the property: the category
W^{\bot 1}
is closed under countable direct sums in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
of finitely
M
-filtered
A
-modules.

In Theorem
1.2
,
(2)
is equivalent to saying that the algebra
\Lambda
is virtually Gorenstein (see Proposition
3.18
) in the sense of Beligiannis;
{\rm Add}(M)
contains finitely generated,
M
-compact and
M
-filtered
A
-modules;
(4)
and
(5)
hold true if the module
W
is the direct sum of finitely generated
A
-modules. Moreover, Theorem
1.2
implies that (TC2) holds for symmetric algebras of finite representation type because
(2)
-
(5)
in Theorem
1.2
are satisfied. This can be seen from a classical result, due to Auslander and Ringel-Tachikawa, that any module over an Artin algebra of finite representation type is a direct sum of finitely generated modules. Thus Theorem
1.2
provides a different approach to studying (TC2).

1.2
Nakayama conjecture for Gorenstein-Morita algebras

As indicated by the relation between (TC2) and (NC), we can apply Theorem
1.2
to discuss (NC) for
strongly Morita
algebras, which are, by definition, the endomorphism algebras of Nakayama-stable generators over self-injective algebras. For this purpose, we focus on two classes of modules that are associated with compact objects in some stable categories. This leads to introducing the notions of compactly Gorenstein algebras and Gorenstein-Morita algebras in terms of these modules.

Definition 1.3
.

Let
B
ba an Artin algebra and
Y
a
B
-module.

(i)
The
B
-module
Y
is
compactly filtered
if it has a filtration

0=Y_{0}\subseteq Y_{1}\subseteq Y_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Y_{n}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Y
of
B
-modules
such that
Y=\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}Y_{n}
and the subquotient
Y_{n+1}/Y_{n}
of
Y
is isomorphic to a finitely generated
B
-module of finite projective dimension for
n\in\mathbb{N}
; and
compactly Gorenstein-projective
if it is a compact object in the stable category of Gorenstein-projective
B
-modules.

(ii)
The algebra
B
is
compactly Gorenstein
if any compactly filtered, compactly Gorenstein-projective
B
-module is projective; and
Gorenstein-Morita
if
B
is both strongly Morita and compactly Gorenstein.

Clearly, finitely generated and compactly filtered modules are exactly finitely generated modules of finite projective dimension, while finitely generated Gorenstein-projective modules are compactly Gorenstein-projective. Compactly Gorenstein algebras include virtually Gorenstein algebras (see
[
11
,
12
]
) and algebras of finite finitistic dimension (see Lemma
5.1
). Moreover, compactly Gorenstein algebras over a field are closed under derived equivalences and stable equivalences of adjoint type by Corollary
5.3
. Since the Nakayama functor of a symmetric algebra is the identity functor, strongly Morita algebras capture
gendo-symmetric algebras
which are, by definition, the endomorphism algebras of generators over symmetric algebras (see
[
21
,
18
]
). Examples of gendo-symmetric algebras include Hecke algebras, (quantized) Schur algebras, and blocks of the Bernstein-Gelfand-Gelfand category
\mathcal{O}
of semisimple complex Lie algebras.

As a consequence of Theorem
1.2
, we show that (NC) holds for Gorenstein-Morita algebras.

Corollary 1.4
.

Let
B
be a Gorenstein-Morita algebra. If
B
has infinite dominant dimension, then it is self-injective.
In particular, any gendo-symmetric, virtually Gorenstein algebra with infinite dominant dimension
is symmetric.

As is known,
not
all Artin algebras are virtually Gorenstein (see
[
14
]
), however, we would like to conjecture that
all Artin algebras are compactly Gorenstein
. If this is true, then (NC) holds for strongly Morita algebras, and in particular for gendo-symmetric algebras.

In the preprint
[
19
]
, we discuss (TC2) for symmetric algebras in terms of recollements of derived module categories and stratifying ideals of algebras. Consequently, it is shown that the validity of (TC2) for symmetric algebras is equivalent to saying that no indecomposable symmetric algebras have stratifying ideals apart from themselves and
0
.

1.3
Recollements of relative stable categories from self-orthogonal modules

To prove Theorem
1.2
, we first investigate arbitrary (not necessarily self-orthogonal) generator over a self-injective algebra, and construct two pairs of triangle endofunctors for stable module categories. This enables us to establish a recollement of the relative stable categories for a self-orthogonal generator. By employing the heart of a torsion pair in the stable module category, we then characterize the compact objects of the right term of this recollement.

Let
A
be a self-injective algebra and
M
a generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. For a full subcategory
\mathscr{X}
of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, we denote by
\mathscr{X}/[M]
the quotient category of
\mathscr{X}
modulo
{\rm Add}(M)
. In particular,
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}/[A]
is the same as the stable module category
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. For simplicity, we denote by
\underline{{\rm Hom}}(X,Y)
the Hom-set in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
for
A
-modules
X
and
Y
, and define
\Gamma:=\underline{{\rm End}}_{A}(M)
, called the
stable endomorphism algebra
of
{{}_{A}}M
. Let

\underline{M}^{\bot}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X[n])=0\;\;\mbox{for all}\;\;n\in\mathbb{Z}\}.

Given the pair
(A,M)
, we construct explicitly two pairs of triangle endofunctors of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
(see Section
3.2
for details):

(\Phi,\Psi)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;(\Phi^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime}):\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}},

and define
\mathscr{S}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\mid\Psi(\underline{X})=0\}.

If
{{}_{A}}M
is additionally self-orthogonal or
\Omega
-periodic (that is,
\Omega_{A}^{n}(X)\simeq X
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
for a positive integer
n
), then
\mathscr{S}
is the smallest thick subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
containing
M
and being closed under direct sums (see Corollary
3.13
), and the above endofunctors contribute to building the recollement of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
in Theorem
3.14
.

Now, suppose that
M
is a self-orthogonal and Nakayama-stable generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. Further, we consider the following two categories associated with
M
:

\mathscr{H}:=\{X\in\underline{\mathscr{S}}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X[n])=0\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;n\neq 0\}\mbox{ and }

\mathscr{E}:=\{X\in\mathscr{G}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X),\,{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M[1],X)\in\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\},

where
\mathscr{H}
is the heart of a torsion pair in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
determined by
M
, and thus an abelian category (see the beginning of Section
4
).

The main result on constructing recollements of relative stable categories reads as follows.

Theorem 1.5
.

Let
A
be a self-injective Artin algebra and
M
a self-orthogonal and Nakayama-stable generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. Then the following hold.

(1)
There exists a recollement of triangulated categories:

\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(2)
The recollement in
(1)
restricts to a recollement of triangulated categories:

\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}/[M]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M].\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(3)

\dim\big((\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]\big)\leq min\{2\;LL(\Gamma)-1,2\;{\rm gl.dim}(\Gamma)+1\}
, where
LL(\Gamma)
and
{\rm gl.dim}(\Gamma)
denote the Loewy length and global dimension of the algebra
\Gamma
, respectively.

Of importance are the compact objects for triangulated categories. Since
\mathscr{C}
is compactly generated, it follows from basic properties of recollements that
\underline{M}^{\bot}
has compact objects. It seems, however, to be unclear that the category
(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
has compact objects. In the following, we gives a complete description of its compact objects (see also Corollary
4.18
for details).

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a triangulated category and
\mathcal{U}
a set of objects in
\mathcal{T}
. For integers
i\leq j
and
n\geq 0
, we denote by
\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle_{n+1}^{[i,j]}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
consisting of all objects obtained by taking
(n+1)
-fold extensions of finite direct sums of objects in the set
\{U[-s]\mid U\in\mathcal{U},\,s\in\mathbb{Z},\,i\leq s\leq j\}
.

Proposition 1.6
.

Let
A
be a self-injective Artin algebra and
M
a self-orthogonal, Nakayama-stable generator. Then the following hold.

(1)
Each object
X
of
\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S}
is
M
-compact and isomorphic in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
to an
M
-filtered module.
Moreover,
X
is finitely generated if and only if
X\in{\rm add}({{}_{A}}M)
.

(2)
The category
(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
is a compactly generated triangulated category and has
(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
as its full subcategory consisting of all compact objects.

(3)
Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of isomorphism classes of simple objects of
\mathscr{H}
, and let
n
be the Loewy length of the algebra
\Gamma
. Then

\big(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}\big)/[M]=\langle{\rm Add}(\mathcal{S})\rangle_{2n}^{[-1,0]}
and

(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]=\langle\mathcal{S}\rangle_{2n}^{[-1,0]}
, where
\mathcal{S}
is a finite set.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.5
, the module

{{}_{A}}M
is projective if and only if
(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]=0
if and only if
(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]=0
(see Corollary
4.9
). Thus (TC2) is true for the pair
(A,M)
exactly when the recollements in Theorem
1.5
are trivial. Hence, to construct a counterexample to (TC2), our results, Theorem
1.5
and Proposition
1.6
, provide necessary homological information on self-orthogonal modules.

1.4
Overview of the contexts

The contents of this article are sketched as follows. In Section
2
we briefly recall definitions of quotient categories, recollements and Gorenstein-projective modules over algebras. In Section
3
we construct two pairs of triangle endofunctors of the stable module category
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
for a self-injective algebra
A
with a generator
{{}_{A}}M
. With these endofunctors, we establish the recollement in Theorem
3.14
of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
determined by
M
. Moreover, we show that the subcategory
\mathscr{G}
of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
relative to
M
is equivalent to the category of Gorenstein-projective modules over the endomorphism algebra of
M
(see Lemma
3.17
). In Section
4
we prove Theorem
1.5
and establish a representability theorem for a series of homological functors (see Theorem
4.14
). In Section
5
we show Theorem
1.2
and Corollary
1.4
.

2
Preliminaries

In this section we briefly recall definitions, basic facts and notation used in this paper.

2.1
Quotient categories and recollements

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an additive category.

A full subcategory
\mathcal{B}
of
\mathcal{C}
is always assumed to be closed under isomorphisms, that is, if

X\in{\mathcal{B}}
and
Y\in\cal C
with
Y\simeq X
, then

Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

Let
X
be an object in
\mathcal{C}
. The full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
consisting of all direct summands of
finite coproducts of copies of
X
is denoted by
{\rm add}(X)
. If
\mathcal{C}
admits
coproducts (that is, coproducts indexed over sets exist in

{\mathcal{C}}
), then
{\rm Add}(X)
denotes the full subcategory of

\mathcal{C}
consisting of all direct summands of coproducts
of copies of
X
. Dually, if
\mathcal{C}
admits products,
then
{\rm Prod}(X)
denotes the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}

consisting of all direct summands of products of copies of

X
.

For morphisms
f:X\to Y
and
g:Y\to Z
in
\mathcal{C}
, the composition of
f
and
g
is written by
fg
, a morphism from

X
to
Z
. The induced morphisms
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(Z,f):{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(Z,X)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(Z,Y)
and

{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(f,Z):{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(Y,Z)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Z)
are denoted by
f^{*}
and
f_{*}
, respectively.

For functors
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
and
G:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
, the composition of
F
and
G
is denoted by
G\circ F
which is a functor from
\mathcal{C}
to

\mathcal{E}
. Let
{\rm Ker}(F)
and
{\rm Im}(F)
be the kernel and image of
the functor
F
, respectively. In particular,
{\rm Ker}(F)
is closed
under isomorphisms in
\mathcal{C}
. In this paper, we require that

{\rm Im}(F)
is closed under isomorphisms in
\mathcal{D}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{B}
is a full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
. A morphism
f:X\to Y
in
\mathcal{C}
is called a

right
\mathcal{B}
-approximation
of
Y
if
X\in\mathcal{B}
and

{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(B,f):{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(B,X)\to{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(B,Y)
is surjective for any
B\in\mathcal{B}
; and

right minimal
if
\alpha\in{\rm End}_{\mathcal{C}}(X)
is an isomorphism whenever
f=\alpha f
.
If
f
is both a right minimal morphism and a right
\mathcal{B}
-approximation of
Y
, then
f
is called a
minimal right
\mathcal{B}
-approximation
of
Y
. In this case, the object
X
is unique up to isomorphism and is called the minimal right
\mathcal{B}
-approximation of
Y
(without mentioning
f
).
If each object of
\mathcal{C}
admits a right
\mathcal{B}
-approximation, then
\mathcal{B}
is said to be
contravariantly finite
in
\mathcal{C}
.
Dually, there are the notions of (minimal) left approximations and
covariantly finite
subcategories in
\mathcal{C}
.
If
\mathcal{B}
is both contravariantly and covariantly finite in
\mathcal{C}
, then it is said to be
functorially finite
in
\mathcal{C}
.

We recall Wakamatsu’s Lemma (see
[
7
, Proposition 1.3]
):
Let
\mathcal{S}
be a class of
R
-modules over a ring
R
closed under extensions. If
f:C\rightarrow X
is a minimal right
\mathcal{S}
-approximation of an
R
-module
X
, then
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{R}(L,{\rm Ker}(f))=0
for
L\in\mathcal{S}
. Dually, if
g:X\rightarrow C^{\prime}
is a minimal left
\mathcal{S}
-approximation of an
R
-module
X
, then
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{R}({\rm Coker}(g),M)=0
for
M\in\mathcal{S}
.

Next, we recall the definition of quotient categories of additive categories.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
. Denote by
\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{D}
the
quotient category
of
\mathcal{C}
modulo
\mathcal{D}
. It has the same objects as
\mathcal{C}
, but its morphism set for any two objects

X
and
Y
is given by
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{D}}(X,Y):={\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)/\mathcal{D}(X,Y)

where
\mathcal{D}(X,Y)
is the subgroup of
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)
consisting of all morphisms
factorizing through objects in
\mathcal{D}
. The canonical quotient functor
q:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{D}

sends a morphism
f:X\to Y
in
\mathcal{C}
to
f+\mathcal{D}(X,Y)
in
\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{D}
.
Clearly,
{\rm Ker}(q)
consists of all direct summands (in
\mathcal{C}
) of objects of
\mathcal{D}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
admits coproducts. An object
X
is said to be
compact
in
\mathcal{C}
if
the functor
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,-)
from
\mathcal{C}
to the category of abelian groups commutes with coproducts.
The full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
consisting of compact objects is denoted by
\mathcal{C}^{\rm c}
.
A set
\mathcal{U}
of objects of
\mathcal{C}
is called a
compact generating set
of
\mathcal{C}
if each object of
\mathcal{U}
is compact in
\mathcal{C}
and an object
X\in\mathcal{C}
is zero whenever
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(U,X)=0
for all
U\in\mathcal{U}
.
When
\mathcal{C}
is a triangulated category, it is said to be
compactly generated
if it has a compact generating set.
If
\mathcal{U}
is a set of compact objects of
\mathcal{C}
closed under shifts, then
\mathcal{U}
is a compact generating set of
\mathcal{C}
if and only if
\mathcal{C}
itself is the smallest full triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
containing
\mathcal{U}
and being closed under coproducts.

The following result is elementary.

Lemma 2.1
.

(1)
Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be additive categories, and let
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
be full subcategories. Suppose that
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
and
G:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
form an adjoint pair
(F,G)
of additive functors.
If
F(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
and
G(\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, then the adjoint pair
(F,G)
induces an adjoint pair
(F_{0},G_{0})
of additive functors
F_{0}:\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{Y}
and
G_{0}:\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{X}
.

(2)
Suppose that an additive category
\mathcal{C}
admits coproducts and
\mathcal{X}
is a full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
closed under coproducts. Then
\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{X}
admits coproducts, and the quotient functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{X}
preserves coproducts and compact objects.

We denote by
{\mathscr{C}}(\mathcal{C})
the category of all complexes over
\mathcal{C}
with chain maps, and
{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{C})
the homotopy category of
{\mathscr{C}}(\mathcal{C})
. For a chain map
f^{\bullet}:X^{\bullet}\to Y^{\bullet}
in
{\mathscr{C}}(\mathcal{C})
, we denote by
{\rm Con}(f^{\bullet})
the mapping cone of
f^{\bullet}
. There is a distinguished triangle
X^{\bullet}\to Y^{\bullet}\to{\rm Con}(f^{\bullet})\to X^{\bullet}[1]
in
{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{C})
. When
\mathcal{C}
is a full subcategory of an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\mathcal{C})
the full subcategory of
{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{A})
consisting of acyclic complexes of
\mathcal{C}
. Clearly, if
f^{\bullet}
is a quasi-isomorphism, then
{\rm Con}(f^{\bullet})
is acyclic. If
\mathcal{C}
is an abelian category, we denote by
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathcal{C})
the
unbounded derived category
of
\mathcal{C}
, which is the localization of
{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{C})
by inverting all quasi-isomorphisms. Clearly,
{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{A})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathcal{C})
are triangulated categories.

Next, we recall the notion of recollements of triangulated
categories, introduced in
[
10
]
for studying derived categories of
perverse sheaves over singular spaces.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{T^{\prime\prime}}
be
triangulated categories.
\mathcal{T}
is called a

recollement
of
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{T^{\prime\prime}}
(or there is a recollement among
\mathcal{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}
) if there
are six triangle functors

\textstyle{\mathcal{T^{\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}=i_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{!}=j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}

among the three categories such
that

(1)

(i^{*},i_{*}),(i_{!},i^{!}),(j_{!},j^{!})
and
(j^{*},j_{*})
are adjoint
pairs,

(2)

i_{*},j_{*}
and
j_{!}
are fully faithful functors,

(3)

j^{!}i_{!}=0
(and thus also
i^{!}j_{*}=0
and
i^{*}j_{!}=0
), and

(4)
for an object
X\in\mathcal{T}
, there are two triangles

i_{!}i^{!}(X)\to X\to j_{*}j^{*}(X)\to i_{!}i^{!}(X)[1]
and
j_{!}j^{!}(X)\to X\to i_{*}i^{*}(X)\to j_{!}j^{!}(X)[1]
in
\mathcal{T}
induced by the counits and units of the adjunctions,
where [
1
] denotes the shift functor of
\mathcal{T}
.

By a
half recollement
among
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{T}

and
\mathcal{T^{\prime\prime}}
, we mean that
i^{*},i_{*},j_{!}
and
j^{!}
satisfy the corresponding properties
(1)
-
(4)
involved in Definition
2.2
. Note that there is a one-to-one correspondence between equivalence classes of half recollements (respectively, recollements) of triangulated categories and hereditary torsion pairs (respectively, TTF triples) of triangulated categories.
Recall that a
torsion pair
in
\mathcal{T}
is a pair
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
of full subcategories
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
of
\mathcal{T}
satisfying the three conditions:

(a)

{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{T}}(X,Y)=0
for
X\in\mathcal{X}
and
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
;

(b)

\mathcal{X}[1]\subseteq\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}[-1]\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
; and

(c)
for any
M\in\mathcal{T}
, there is a triangle

X\to M\to Y\to X[1]
in
\mathcal{T}
with
X\in\mathcal{X}
and
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
.

A torsion pair
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
of
\mathcal{T}
is said to be
hereditary
if
\mathcal{X}
(or equivalently,
\mathcal{Y})
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
.
In this case, the inclusion
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{T}
has a right adjoint, the inclusion
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{T}
has a left adjoint, and there is a half recollement among
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{Y}
. If
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z})
are hereditary torsion pairs in
\mathcal{T}
, then
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z})
is called a TTF
(torsion-torsionfree) triple
in
\mathcal{T}
. In this case, there is a recollement among
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{Y}
. Conversely, the recollement in Definition
2.2
gives a TTF triple
({\rm Im}(j_{!}),{\rm Im}(i_{*}),{\rm Im}(j_{*}))
in
\mathcal{T}
.
For more details, see
[
33
, Chap. 9]
,
[
13
, Chap. I. 2]
or
[
17
, Section 2.3]
.

Hereditary torsion pairs can be constructed in compactly generated triangulated categories as follows.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category. Then coproducts and products indexed by sets exist in
\mathcal{T}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}
be a set of objects in
\mathcal{T}
. Denote by
{\rm Loc}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})
,
{\rm Coloc}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})
and
{\rm thick}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})
the smallest full triangulated subcategories of
\mathcal{T}
containing
\mathcal{S}
and being closed under coproducts, products and direct summands, respectively. If
\mathcal{T}
is clearly understood in the context, we shall write
{\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S})
,
{\rm Coloc}(\mathcal{S})
and
{\rm thick}(\mathcal{S})
for
{\rm Loc}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})
,
{\rm Coloc}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})
and
{\rm thick}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})
, respectively.
By
\mathcal{S}^{\bot}
we denote the right
orthogonal full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
with respect to
\mathcal{S}
, that is,
\mathcal{S}^{\bot}=\{C\in\mathcal{T}\mid{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{T}}(S[n],C)=0,\forall\;S\in\mathcal{S},n\in\mathbb{Z}\}.
Then
\mathcal{S}^{\bot}
is a triangulated subcategory of

\mathcal{T}
closed under products. Similarly,
{{}^{\bot}}\mathcal{S}

stands for the left orthogonal full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
with respect
to
\mathcal{S}
.

In general, the opposite category of a compactly generated triangulated category is not compactly generated, but is perfectly generated in the sense of Krause (see
[
29
]
).
It is worth mentioning that perfectly generated triangulated categories not only generalize compactly generated triangulated categories, but also satisfy the Brown representability theorem in
[
29
, Theorem A]
. This implies the following result:

If a triangle functor from a perfectly generated triangulated category to another triangulated category preserves coproducts, then it has a right adjoint.

The next result seems to be known. For the convenience of the reader, we include here a proof.

Proposition 2.3
.

If
\mathcal{T}
is a compactly generated triangulated category and
\mathcal{S}
is a set of objects in
\mathcal{T}
, then
({\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S}),\mathcal{S}^{\bot})
and
({{}^{\bot}}\mathcal{S},{\rm Coloc}(\mathcal{S}))
are hereditary torsion pairs in
\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.
The Verdier localization
Q_{\mathcal{S}}:\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{T}/{\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S})
preserves coproducts
and
{\rm Ker}(Q_{\mathcal{S}})={\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S})
. Since
\mathcal{T}
is compactly generated, it is perfectly generated. It follows that
Q_{\mathcal{S}}
has a right adjoint. Then
({\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S}),{\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S})^{\bot})
is a torsion pair in
\mathcal{T}
by
[
33
, Theorem 9.1.13]
. Since
{\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S})^{\bot}=\mathcal{S}^{\bot}
,

({\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S}),\mathcal{S}^{\bot})
is a torsion pair in
\mathcal{T}
. Moreover, it is hereditary because
{\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S})
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
.
This proof also implies that
({\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S}),\mathcal{S}^{\bot})
is a hereditary torsion pair in
\mathcal{T}
whenever
\mathcal{T}
is perfectly generated.

Clearly,
{\rm Coloc}(\mathcal{S})
can be regarded as a localizing subcategory of
\mathcal{T}^{\rm op}
, that is,
{\rm Coloc}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{S})={\rm Loc}_{\mathcal{T}^{\rm op}}(\mathcal{S}^{\rm op})
, where
\mathcal{S}^{\rm op}:=\{X^{\rm op}\in\mathcal{T}^{\rm op}\mid X\in\mathcal{S}\}
. Since
\mathcal{T}^{\rm op}
is perfectly generated,
({\rm Loc}(\mathcal{S}^{\rm op}),{(\mathcal{S}^{\rm op})}^{\bot})
is a hereditary torsion pair in
\mathcal{T}^{\rm op}
, that is,
({{}^{\bot}}\mathcal{S},{\rm Coloc}(\mathcal{S}))
is a hereditary torsion pair in
\mathcal{T}
.
\square

Let
F
,
G
and
H
be triangle endofunctors of
\mathcal{T}
. We say that a sequence of natural transformations

F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta}}{{\longrightarrow}}H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}F[1]:\;\;\mathcal{T}\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}

is
exact
if for each
X\in\mathcal{T}

the sequence
F(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}G(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}F(X)[1]
is a triangle in
\mathcal{T}
.

2.2
Cotorsion pairs and Gorestein-projective modules

In the subsection we recall the definitions of cotorsion pairs in abelian categories and Gorenstein-projective modules over algebras.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian category and
n\geq 1
a natural number. Given a class
\mathcal{S}
of objects in
\mathcal{A}
, we define

{{}^{\bot n}}\mathcal{S}:=\{X\in\mathcal{A}\mid{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{n}(X,S)=0\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;S\in\mathcal{S}\},\;\;{{}^{\bot>0}}\mathcal{S}:=\bigcap_{n\geq 1}{{}^{\bot n}}\mathcal{S},

\mathcal{S}^{\bot n}:=\{X\in\mathcal{A}\mid{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{n}(S,X)=0\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;S\in\mathcal{S}\}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\mathcal{S}^{\bot>0}:=\bigcap_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{S}{{}^{\bot n}}.

Definition 2.4
.

(1)
A pair
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})
of full subcategories of
\mathcal{A}
is called a
cotorsion pair
in
\mathcal{A}
if

(i)

\mathcal{U}={{}^{\bot 1}}\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{U}^{\bot 1}
;

(ii)
For each object
X\in\mathcal{A}
, there are exact sequences

0\to V_{X}\to U_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X\to 0
and
0\to X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}V^{X}\to U^{X}\to 0

in
\mathcal{A}
such that
U_{X},U^{X}\in\mathcal{U}
and
V_{X},V^{X}\in\mathcal{V}
.

(2)
A cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})
in
\mathcal{A}
is
hereditary
if
\mathcal{U}={{}^{\bot>0}}\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{U}^{\bot>0}
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})
be a cotorsion pair in
\mathcal{A}
. Then
\pi_{X}
is a right
\mathcal{U}
-approximation of
X
and
\lambda_{X}
is a left
\mathcal{V}
-approximation of
X
. Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{V}
. Then
U_{X}
and
V^{X}
are unique up to isomorphism in the quotient category
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{E}
.
Further, the inclusion
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{E}
has a right adjoint sending
X
to
U_{X}
, while the inclusion
\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{E}
has a left adjoint sending
X
to
V^{X}
. Moreover,
X
has a
\mathcal{U}
-resolution and a
\mathcal{V}
-coresolution in the following sense.

Definition 2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a full subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
. A
\mathcal{C}
-resolution
of an object
U\in\mathcal{A}
is a complex
X^{\bullet}:=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in{\mathscr{C}}(\mathcal{C})
with
X^{i}=0
for all
i\geq 1
, together with a chain map
\pi^{\bullet}:X^{\bullet}\to U
(regarded as a stalk complex), such that
{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}}(C,\pi^{\bullet}):{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}}(C,X^{\bullet})\to{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}}(C,U)
is a quasi-isomorphism
for any
C\in\mathcal{C}
. In this case,
\pi^{\bullet}
is called a
\mathcal{C}
-resolution of
U
for simplicity. Dually, a
\mathcal{C}
-coresolution
of
U
can be defined.

Let
A
be an Artin algebra. We denote by
A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
and
A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
the full subcategories of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
consisting of projective and injective
A
-modules, respectively.
Moreover, we write
{\mathscr{K}}(A)
,
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)
and
{\mathscr{D}}(A)
for
{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}})
,
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}})
, respectively.

An
A
-module
X
is said to be
Gorenstein-projective
if there is an exact complex

P^{\bullet}:\cdots\to P^{-2}\to P^{-1}\to P^{0}\to P^{1}\to P^{2}\to\cdots
of projective
A
-modules
such that
X\simeq{\rm Ker}(P^{0}\to P^{1})
and the complex
{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{A}(P^{\bullet},A)
is exact.
The complex
P^{\bullet}
is called a
complete projective resolution
of
X
. Dually, one can define Gorenstein-injective modules and complete injective coresolutions. Let
A\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}
and
A\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
be the full subcategories of all Gorenstein-projective and Gorenstein-injective
A
-modules, respectively. By
[
13
, Theorem X. 2.4]
,
(A\mbox{{\rm-GProj}},A\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}^{\bot>0})
and
({{}^{\bot>0}}A\mbox{{\rm-GInj}},A\mbox{{\rm-GInj}})
are hereditary cotorsion pairs in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Moreover, each
A
-module admits a minimal right
A\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}
-approximation and also a minimal left
A\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
-approximation by
[
11
, Proposition 3.8(iv) and Corollary 6.8]
.

An Artin algebra
A
is said to be
virtually Gorenstein
(see
[
11
, Definition 8.1]
) if
A\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}^{\bot>0}={{}^{\bot>0}}A\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
.
Virtually Gorenstein algebras include Gorenstein algebras and algebras of finite representation type, and are closed under derived equivalences and stable equivalences of Morita type.

By
filtered colimits
of
A
-modules we mean colimits of filtered diagrams
I\to A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
with
I
an essentially small, filtered category.

3
Recollements of stable module categories

In this section, we investigate an arbitrary (not necessarily self-orthogonal) generator over a self-injective algebra, and construct two pairs of triangle endofunctors for the stable module category by means of the endomorphism algebra of the generator. When the generator is additionally self-orthogonal or
\Omega
-periodic, we show that these endofunctors coincidentally appear in a recollement of the stable module category determined by the generator (see Theorem
3.14
and Corollary
3.15
). This recollement will be restricted to the one of a relative Gorenstein stable category in the next section. Finally, we describe the category of Gorenstein-projective modules over the endomorphism algebra of a self-orthogonal generator in term of a relative Gorenstein category (see Lemma
3.17
), and provide equivalent characterizations for the endomorphism algebra to be virtually Gorenstein (see Proposition
3.18
).

Throughout this section, let
A
denote a
self-injective Artin algebra
. Then
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}

is a triangulated category with a shift functor
[1]:A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, given by the cosyzygy functor
\Omega^{-}_{A}
.
Clearly,
{\rm Ext}_{A}^{n}(X_{1},X_{2})\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X_{1},X_{2}[n])
for all
n\geq 1
and
X_{1},X_{2}\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Let
q:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
be the canonical functor. By Lemma
2.1
(2) and its dual,
q
preserves direct sums and direct products.
To emphasize objects in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, the image of
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
under
q
is denoted by
\underline{X}
.

A full subcategory
\mathcal{U}
of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is called a
thick subcategory
if it is closed under direct summands in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
and has
the
two out of three property
: if two terms of an exact sequence
0\to X\to Y\to Z\to 0
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
belong to
\mathcal{U}
, then so does the third. If
\mathcal{U}
contains all projective
A
-modules, then
\mathcal{U}
is a thick subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
if and only if
\underline{\mathcal{U}}
is a full triangulated subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

From now on, let
M=A\oplus M_{0}
be a generator in
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
such that
M_{0}
contains no nonzero projective direct summands. As
M
is finitely generated, we know
{\rm Add}(M)={\rm Prod}(M)
.

3.1
Endomorphism algebras of generators over self-injective algebras

In this subsection, we establish additive equivalences between the subcategories of the module category of a self-injective algebra and the ones of the module category of the endomorphism algebra of a generator over the algebra.

Let
\Lambda={\rm End}_{A}(M)
and
e
be the idempotent element of
\Lambda
such that
\Lambda e={\rm Hom}_{A}(M,A)
. Then
A\simeq e\Lambda e
as algebras. Let
S_{e}:\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}},Y\mapsto eY
be the
Schur functor
determined by
e
.
This functor has a fully faithful left and right adjoint functors
F:=\Lambda e\otimes_{A}-
and
G:={\rm Hom}_{A}(e\Lambda,-)
from
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
to
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, respectively. Note that both
F
and
G
commute with direct products and direct sums. Due to
e\Lambda\simeq M
as
A
-
\Lambda
-bimodules, the functor
G
can be identified with
{\rm Hom}_{A}(M,-)
. Moreover, there is a canonical natural transformation
\delta:F\to G

defined by

\delta_{X}:\;F(X)\longrightarrow G(X),\quad ae\otimes x\mapsto[eb\mapsto ebaex]

where
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
,
a,b\in\Lambda
and
x\in X
. For a full subcategory
\mathcal{X}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}

and
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}

we define

{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\mathcal{X}):=\{X^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{X})\mid X^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\mathcal{Y}):=\{Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{Y})\mid S_{e}(Y^{\bullet})\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\}.

Since
S_{e}
is exact,
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\mathcal{Y})
consists of those
Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{Y})
with
H^{i}(Y^{\bullet})\in(\Lambda/\Lambda e\Lambda)\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Given an Artin algebra
B
, we denote by
\nu_{B}
and
\nu_{B}^{-}
the Nakayama functor
D(B)\otimes_{B}-:B\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to B\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
and its right adjoint functor
{\rm Hom}_{B}(D(B),-)
, respectively. By restriction to
B\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, we have natural isomorphisms
\nu_{B}\simeq D\circ{\rm Hom}_{B}(-,B)
and
\nu_{B}^{-}\simeq{\rm Hom}_{B^{\rm op}}(-,B)\circ D
.
As
D(B)
is finitely presented,
{\rm Hom}_{B}(D(B),-)
commutes with filtered colimits, while
D(B)\otimes_{B}-
commutes always with filtered colimits. Since a projective
B
-module is always a direct sum of finitely generated projective
B
-modules (see
[
1
, Theorem 27.11, p.306]
, we can show by projective presentations of modules that, for a self-injective Artin algebra
B
, both
\nu_{B}
and
\nu_{B}^{-}
are exact and auto-equivalent on
B\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.

Lemma 3.1
.

(1)
The restriction of
\delta
to
A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
is a natural isomorphism, that is,
\delta_{X}
is an isomorphism for any
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
.

(2)
There are natural isomorphisms of additive functors:

D\circ F\circ\nu_{A}\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(-,M):A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}},

\nu_{\Lambda}\circ G\simeq F\circ\nu_{A}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\nu_{\Lambda}^{-}\circ F\simeq G\circ\nu_{A}^{-}:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}.

In particular,
F(\nu_{A}(M))\simeq D(\Lambda)
as
\Lambda
-
\Lambda
-bimodules.

(3)
The functor
S_{e}
restricts to equivalences of additive categories:

{\rm Add}(\Lambda e)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}},\;\;\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Add}({{}_{A}}M)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M)).

(4)
Let
\mathcal{X}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
and
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
be additive, full subcategories. If the functor
S_{e}
induces an equivalence
\mathcal{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{X}
of additive categories, then there is an equivalence
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\mathcal{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\mathcal{X})

of triangulated categories.

Proof.

(1)
holds because
\delta_{A}
is an isomorphism and both
F
and
G
commute with direct sums.

(2)
We need the following result, its proof is left to the reader:

(\ast)
Let
A_{1},A_{2}
and
A_{3}
be Artin algebras and let
F_{1},F_{2}:A_{1}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to A_{2}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
be (covariant) additive functors. If
X
is an
A_{1}
-
A_{3}
-bimodule, then
F_{1}(X)
is an
A_{2}
-
A_{3}
-bimodule, where the right
A_{3}
-module structure is given by the composition of associated ring homomorphisms
A_{3}\to{\rm End}_{A_{1}}(X)
and
{\rm End}_{A_{1}}(X)\to{\rm End}_{A_{2}}(F_{1}(X))
. Additionally, if
\eta:F_{1}\to F_{2}
is a natural transformation, then
\eta_{X}:F_{1}(X)\to F_{2}(X)
is a homomorphism of
A_{2}
-
A_{3}
-bimodules.

Note that
D\circ(F\circ\nu_{A})=D\big(\Lambda e\otimes_{A}\nu_{A}(-)\big)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(\nu_{A}(-),D(\Lambda e))
and
D(\Lambda e)=D{\rm Hom}_{A}(M,A)\simeq D(A)\otimes_{A}M=\nu_{A}(M)
as
A
-
\Lambda
-bimodules by
(\ast)
. Since
\nu_{A}:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is an auto-equivalence, there are isomorphisms

D\circ(F\circ\nu_{A})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}\big(\nu_{A}(-),\nu_{A}(M)\big)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(-,M):A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. This implies
D\circ(F\circ\nu_{A})(M)\simeq\Lambda
as
\Lambda^{\rm op}
-modules. Since
M
is an
A
-
\Lambda
-bimodule, it follows from
(\ast)
that
D\circ(F\circ\nu_{A})(M)\simeq\Lambda
as
\Lambda
-
\Lambda
-bimodules. This leads to
D(\Lambda)\simeq F(\nu_{A}(M))
as
\Lambda
-
\Lambda
-bimodules. Consequently, there are natural isomorphisms:

\nu_{\Lambda}\circ G=D(\Lambda)\otimes_{\Lambda}G(-)\simeq\Lambda e\otimes_{A}D(A)\otimes_{A}M\otimes_{\Lambda}{\rm Hom}_{A}(M,-)\simeq\Lambda e\otimes_{A}D(A)\otimes_{A}-=F\circ\nu_{A},

\nu_{\Lambda}^{-}\circ F\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda e\otimes_{A}\nu_{A}(M),\Lambda e\otimes_{A}-)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(\nu_{A}(M),-)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(M,\nu_{A}^{-}(-))=G\circ\nu_{A}^{-}.

(3)
The restrictions of
S_{e}
and
G
to the corresponding subcategories give the first and second equivalences since
S_{e}
and
G
commutes with direct sums. Now, we claim that
(F,S_{e})
restricts to equivalences between
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
and
{\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M))
.

In fact,
S_{e}(D(\Lambda_{\Lambda}))=eD(\Lambda)\simeq D(\Lambda e)=\nu_{A}(M)
. As
S_{e}
commutes with direct sums and
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}={\rm Add}(D(\Lambda_{\Lambda}))={\rm Prod}(D(\Lambda_{\Lambda}))
, the Schur functor

S_{e}
restricts to a functor from
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
to
{\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M))
. Moreover, by
(2)
,
F(\nu_{A}(M))\simeq D(\Lambda)
as
\Lambda
-modules.
As
F
commutes with direct sums, it also restricts to a functor from
{\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M))
onto
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
.
Recall that
(F,S_{e})
is an adjoint pair and
F
is fully faithful. Thus
F:{\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M))\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
is an equivalence with the quasi-inverse
S_{e}
.

(4)
Since
S_{e}:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
is an equivalence, it induces an equivalence
{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{Y})\simeq{\mathscr{K}}(\mathcal{X})
.
Then
(4)
follows from the definition of
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\mathcal{Y})
.
\square

Remark 3.2
.

(1)
Let
{\bf P}_{1}(\Lambda e)
(respectively,
{\bf I}_{1}(\Lambda e)
) be the full subcategory of
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
consisting of all modules
Y
such that there is an exact sequence
E_{1}\to E_{0}\to Y\to 0
(respectively,
0\to Y\to E_{0}\to E_{1}
) in
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
with
E_{0},E_{1}\in{\rm Add}(\Lambda e)
. By
[
3
, Lemma 3.1]
, there are additive equivalences

F:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to{\bf P}_{1}(\Lambda e)
and
G:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to{\bf I}_{1}(\Lambda e).

Thus, by Lemma
3.1
(2), the functor
\nu_{\Lambda}
restricts to an additive equivalence

{\bf I}_{1}(\Lambda e)\to{\bf P}_{1}(\Lambda e)
, with the inverse
\nu_{\Lambda}^{-}
.

(2)
Each
A
-module admits a minimal right and left
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximations.
This property will be used in Section
3.4
.

Indeed, since
\Lambda
is an Artin algebra, each
\Lambda
-module admits a projective cover. For any
A
-module
X
, let
f_{X}:Q\to G(X)
be a projective cover of
{{}_{\Lambda}}G(X)
. Since
G
is fully faithful and restricts to an equivalence from
{\rm Add}(M)
to
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
by Lemma
3.1
(3), there is a homomorphism
r_{X}:M_{X}\to X
of
A
-modules such that
M_{X}\in{\rm Add}(M)
and
G(r_{X})=f_{X}
. Then
r_{X}
is a minimal right
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation of
X
.

Let
\overline{F}=F\circ\nu_{A}:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
be the composition of
\nu_{A}
with
F
. Then
\overline{F}
is fully faithful. By Lemma
3.1
(3),
\overline{F}
restricts to an equivalence from
{\rm Add}(M)
to
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
. Thus, if
g_{X}:\overline{F}(X)\to I
is an injective envelop of
\overline{F}(X)
, then there is a homomorphism
\ell_{X}:X\to M^{X}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
with
M^{X}\in{\rm Add}(M)
such that
g_{X}=\overline{F}(\ell_{X})
. One can check that
\ell_{X}
is a minimal left
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation of
X
.

Finally, we recall some notation and facts on homotopy and derived categories of rings.

Let
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
(respectively,
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}
) be the smallest full
triangulated subcategory of
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)
which

(i)
contains all bounded above (respectively, bounded below)
complexes of projective (respectively, injective)
\Lambda
-modules, and

(ii)
is closed under arbitrary direct sums (respectively, direct
products).

Note that
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
and
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
. Moreover, the compositions

{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)
and
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)
are equivalences.
This means that, for any
Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)
, there exists a complex
{{}_{p}}Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
together
with a quasi-isomorphism
{{}_{p}}Y^{\bullet}\to Y^{\bullet}
. Dually, there is a complex
{{}_{i}}Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}
together with a quasi-isomorphism

Y^{\bullet}\to{{}_{i}}Y^{\bullet}
. Moreover,
{{}_{p}}Y^{\bullet}
and
{{}_{i}}Y^{\bullet}
are unique up to isomorphism in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)
. As usual,

{{}_{p}}Y^{\bullet}
and
{{}_{i}}Y^{\bullet}
are called the
projective resolution
and
injective coresolution
of
Y^{\bullet}
in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)
, respectively.
For example, if
Y
is a
\Lambda
-module, then
{{}_{p}}Y
is a deleted projective resolution of
Y
.

Let
Q:{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)
be the localization functor.
Then
Q
has a left adjoint
Q_{\lambda}
and a right adjoint
Q_{\rho}
defined by

Q_{\lambda}={{}_{p}}(-):\;\;{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)\quad\mbox{and}\quad Q_{\rho}={{}_{i}}(-):\;\;{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda).

It is known that
{\rm Im}(Q_{\lambda})={\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
and
{\rm Im}(Q_{\rho})={\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

[
30
]

({\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P},{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}))
and
({\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}),{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I})
are hereditary torsion pairs in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
and
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
, respectively. In other words, there are half recollements of triangulated categories:

\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\scriptstyle{I_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q_{\lambda}}

and

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q_{\rho}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J_{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J}

where
I
and
J
are the inclusion functors, and
Q
denotes restrictions of the localization functor.

3.2
Construction of triangle endofunctors of stable module categories

In the subsection we introduce two pairs of triangle endofunctors of stable module categories for self-injective algebras,
and then discuss two relevant thick subcategories of module categories.

Let
\epsilon:Q_{\lambda}\circ Q\to{\rm Id}
and
\eta:{\rm Id}\to I\circ I_{\lambda}
be the counit and unit adjunctions with respect to the adjoint pairs
(Q_{\lambda},Q)
and
(I_{\lambda},I)
in Lemma
3.3
, respectively, where
{\rm Id}
denotes the identity functor.

Lemma 3.4
.

(1)
There is a half recollement of triangulated categories:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 29.60423pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.60423pt\raise 41.63174pt\raise-5.39166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{I}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.74745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.74745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.58046pt\raise-6.07222pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.71112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Q_{\lambda}\circ Q}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 163.34917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.84354pt\raise 20.32219pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.86113pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{I_{\lambda}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 15.94603pt\raise 7.9468pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 163.34917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 147.67365pt\raise 20.95421pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.35417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 114.23071pt\raise 7.94113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\rm{inc}
is the inclusion functor.

(2)
There is an exact sequence of triangle endofunctors of
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
:

Q_{\lambda}\circ Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Id}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta}}{{\longrightarrow}}I\circ I_{\lambda}\longrightarrow Q_{\lambda}\circ Q[1]

Proof.
Since
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
and
({\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P},{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}))
is a hereditary torsion pair in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
, the pair
({\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\cap{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P},{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}))
is a hereditary torsion pair in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
. By Lemma
3.3
, each object
Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
is endowed with a canonical triangle

Q_{\lambda}\circ Q(Y^{\bullet})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon_{Y^{\bullet}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta_{Y^{\bullet}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}I\circ I_{\lambda}(Y^{\bullet})\longrightarrow Q_{\lambda}\circ Q(Y^{\bullet})[1].

If
Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
, then it follows from
I\circ I_{\lambda}(Y^{\bullet})\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
and the exactness of
S_{e}
that

Q_{\lambda}\circ Q(Y^{\bullet})\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\cap{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
. Thus
(1)
and
(2)
hold.
\square

Now let
\ell:{\mathscr{K}}(A)\to{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
be a
left adjoint
of the inclusion
{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}(A)
.
By
[
15
, Corollary 1.3]
,
\ell
is given by taking the total complexes of Cartan-Eilenberg injective coresolutions of complexes over
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Moreover, it has the following property.

Lemma 3.5
.

The functor
\ell
restricts to a triangle functor
\ell_{ac}:{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\to{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
and the composition

\ell_{M}:\;\;{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ell_{ac}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})={\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}).

is a left adjoint of the inclusion
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))
.

Proof.
Clearly,
({\rm Ker}(\ell),{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}))
and
({\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A),{\mathscr{K}}(A)_{I})
are hereditary torsion pairs in
{\mathscr{K}}(A)
. Since
{\mathscr{K}}(A)_{I}\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
, we have
{\rm Ker}(\ell)\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)
. This implies that
({\rm Ker}(\ell),{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}))
is a hereditary torsion pair in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)
. Consequently,
\ell
restricts to a functor
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\to{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
. Since
{{}_{A}}M
is a generator,
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))
.
Now the second part of Lemma
3.5
holds because
\ell
is a left adjoint of the inclusion
{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})\to{\mathscr{K}}(A)
.
\square

Let
X
be an
A
-module. We denote by
\pi^{\bullet}_{X}:P^{\bullet}_{X}\to X
and
\lambda^{\bullet}_{X}:X\to I^{\bullet}_{X}

a minimal projective resolution and injective coresolution of
X
, respectively. Then there exists a triangle equivalence

S:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}),\quad X\mapsto S(X):={\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{X}\lambda^{\bullet}_{X}).

This functor
S
is called the
stabilization functor
of
A
(for example, see
[
30
]
), while
S(X)
is a complete projective resolution of
X
.
A quasi-inverse of
S
is given by taking the
0
-th cocycle of complexes:

Z^{0}:{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}},\quad I^{\bullet}\mapsto Z^{0}(I^{\bullet}):={\rm Ker}(I^{0}\to I^{1}).

Further, let
\ell_{M}:{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))\to{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
be the triangle functor defined in Lemma
3.5
, and let
\mu:{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}({\rm Add}(\Lambda e))\to{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
be the inclusion induced from
{\rm Add}(\Lambda e)\subseteq\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
.

By Lemmas
3.4
and
3.5
, we can define a pair of triangle endofunctors of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
by

\Phi=Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}\circ S_{e}\circ(Q_{\lambda}\circ Q)\circ\mu\circ G\circ S:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}},

\Psi=Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}\circ S_{e}\circ(I\circ I_{\lambda})\circ\mu\circ G\circ S:\;\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}.

They are illustrated by the following diagram

\textstyle{A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Id}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}({\rm Add}(\Lambda e))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Id}
\scriptstyle{Q_{\lambda}\circ Q}
\scriptstyle{I\circ I_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})}
\scriptstyle{Z^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{M}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})}
\scriptstyle{S_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

where the equivalences of
G
and
S_{e}
follow from Lemma
3.1
(3)-(4).
There is the natural isomorphism

Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}\circ S_{e}\circ{\rm Id}\circ\mu\circ G\circ S\simeq{\rm Id}:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}.

This follows from the equivalence

S_{e}:\;{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}({\rm Add}(\Lambda e))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})

given by Lemma
3.1
(3)-(4), together with the fact that the restriction of
\ell_{M}
to
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
is isomorphic to Id.

Dually, we can construct another pair
(\Phi^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime})
of endofunctors of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. Here, we only list some key points of this construction, and omit the details.

By Lemma
3.3
, there is a half recollement of triangulated categories:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 47.16223pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-47.16223pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 173.79602pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Q_{\rho}\circ Q}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 18.40875pt\raise 7.99847pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 173.79602pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm
