0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 149.61955pt\raise 20.99171pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 123.97351pt\raise 7.99974pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

This implies an exact sequence of endofunctors of
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
:

J\circ J_{\rho}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Id}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\rho}\circ Q\longrightarrow J\circ J_{\rho}[1]:\;\;{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})

where
\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime}
are counit and unit adjunctions of the adjoint pairs
(J,J_{\rho})
and
(Q,Q_{\rho})
, respectively (see Lemma
3.3
). Let
r:{\mathscr{K}}(A)\to{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
be a right adjoint of the inclusion
{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\to{\mathscr{K}}(A)
. It is given by taking the total direct product complexes of Cartan-Eilenberg projective resolutions of complexes over
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, due to
[
15
, Corollary 1.3]
.
Moreover, it restricts to a triangle functor
r_{ac}:{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\to{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
. Let

r_{M}:\;\;{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M)))\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r_{ac}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})

be the composition of the inclusion with
r_{ac}
, and let
\mu^{\prime}:{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}({\rm Add}(\Lambda e))\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})

be the inclusion induced from
{\rm Add}(\Lambda e)\subseteq\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
.
Now we define the two endofunctors
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Psi^{\prime}
of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
by

\Phi^{\prime}=Z^{0}\circ r_{M}\circ S_{e}\circ(Q_{\rho}\circ Q)\circ\mu^{\prime}\circ F\circ S:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}},

\Psi^{\prime}=Z^{0}\circ r_{M}\circ S_{e}\circ(J\circ J_{\rho})\circ\mu^{\prime}\circ F\circ S:\;\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}},

which are illustrated by the diagram

\textstyle{A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Id}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}({\rm Add}(\Lambda e))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Id}
\scriptstyle{J\circ J_{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{Q_{\rho}\circ Q}
\textstyle{A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})}
\scriptstyle{Z^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(\nu_{A}(M)))}
\scriptstyle{r_{M}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}).}
\scriptstyle{S_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

Similarly, there is a natural isomorphism of functors:

Z^{0}\circ r_{M}\circ S_{e}\circ{\rm Id}\circ\mu^{\prime}\circ F\circ S\simeq{\rm Id}:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}.

By construction,
\Phi
and
\Psi
commute with direct sums, while
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Psi^{\prime}
commute with direct products. Clearly, if
M=A
, then
\Phi=\Phi^{\prime}=0

and
\Psi={\rm Id}=\Psi^{\prime}
. In general, we have the result.

Proposition 3.6
.

There exist exact sequences of triangle endofunctors of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
:

\Phi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\epsilon}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Id}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\eta}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Psi\longrightarrow\Phi[1]\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Psi^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\epsilon^{\prime}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Id}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\eta^{\prime}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Phi^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Psi^{\prime}[1],

where
\widetilde{\epsilon}
,
\widetilde{\eta}
,
\widetilde{\epsilon^{\prime}}
and
\widetilde{\eta^{\prime}}

are induced from
\epsilon
,
\eta
,
\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime}
, respectively.

Next, we investigate two full subcategories of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
associated with the functors
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
.

\mathscr{S}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\mid\Psi(\underline{X})=0\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathscr{T}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\mid\Psi^{\prime}(\underline{X})=0\}.

Since
\Psi
is a triangle functor commutating with direct sums,
\mathscr{S}
is a thick subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
containing projective modules and being closed under direct sums. Dually,
\mathscr{T}
is a thick subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
containing projective modules and being closed under direct products. Also,
\underline{\mathscr{S}}
and
\underline{\mathscr{T}}
are full triangulated subcategories of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

Recall that an
A
-module
X
is said to be
\Omega
-periodic
if
\underline{\Omega_{A}^{n}(X)}\simeq\underline{X}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
for a positive integer
n
.

Lemma 3.7
.

(1)
If
X\in{\rm Add}({{}_{A}}M)
is
\Omega
-periodic, then
X\in\mathscr{S}
and
\nu_{A}(X)\in\mathscr{T}
.

(2)
If
\Lambda
has finite global dimension, then both
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
are isomorphic to the identity functor, and therefore
\mathscr{S}=\mathscr{T}=A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.

Proof.
For an
A
-module
X
, we write
G\circ S(X)=Y^{\bullet}:=(Y^{n},d^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
F\circ S(\nu_{A}(X))=Z^{\bullet}:=(Z^{n},h^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}.

Then
Y^{\bullet},Z^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}({\rm Add}(\Lambda e))
. Since the
\Lambda
-module

\Lambda e
is projective-injective, both
Y^{n}
and
Z^{n}
are projective-injective. As
G
is left exact,
{\rm Ker}(d^{n})=G(\Omega_{A}^{-n}(X))
. Dually,

{\rm Coker}(h^{n})=F\circ\Omega_{A}^{-(n+2)}(\nu_{A}(X))
since
F
is right exact.

(1)
Suppose
X\in{\rm Add}(M)
and
X\simeq\Omega_{A}^{s}(X)
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
for some
s\geq 1
. Then
X\simeq\Omega_{A}^{-sm}(X)
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
G(X)\in\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
by Lemma
3.1
(3). It follows that
{\rm Ker}(d^{sm})=G(\Omega_{A}^{-sm}(X))\simeq G(X)={\rm Ker}(d^{0})\in\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
. Here, the isomorphism is regarded in the stable category.
Let
\tau_{\leq sm}(Y^{\bullet})
be the subcomplex of
Y^{\bullet}
:

\cdots\longrightarrow Y^{-1}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow Y^{sm-2}\longrightarrow Y^{sm-1}\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(d^{sm})\longrightarrow 0.

Then
\tau_{\leq sm}(Y^{\bullet})\in{\mathscr{K}}^{-}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
. Since

Y^{\bullet}
is isomorphic in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
to the homotopy colimit of the sequence of inclusions:

\tau_{\leq 0}(Y^{\bullet})\hookrightarrow\tau_{\leq s}(Y^{\bullet})\hookrightarrow\tau_{\leq 2s}(Y^{\bullet})\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow\tau_{\leq sm}(Y^{\bullet})\hookrightarrow\cdots,

there is a canonical triangle in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
:

\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\tau_{\leq sm}(Y^{\bullet})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\tau_{\leq sm}(Y^{\bullet})\longrightarrow Y^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\tau_{\leq sm}(Y^{\bullet}).

As
{\mathscr{K}}^{-}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
and

{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
is closed under direct sums in

{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
, we get
Y^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
.
Since
({\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P},{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}))
is a hereditary torsion pair in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
, we obtain
Q_{\lambda}\circ Q(Y^{\bullet})\simeq Y^{\bullet}
and

I\circ I_{\lambda}(Y^{\bullet})=0
. Thus
\Psi(\underline{X})=0
and
X\in\mathscr{S}.

Set
U:=\nu_{A}(X)
. It follows from
X\in{\rm Add}(M)
and Lemma
3.1
(3) that
F(U)\in\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
. Since
X\simeq\Omega_{A}^{-s}(X)
for some
s\geq 1
and
\nu_{A}
is an auto-equivalence on
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, we have
U\simeq\Omega_{A}^{sm}(U)
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus
{\rm Coker}(h^{-2-sm})=F\circ\Omega_{A}^{sm}(U)\simeq F(U)={\rm Coker}(h^{-2})
and the truncated quotient complex of
Z^{\bullet}

\tau_{\geq-1-sm}(Z^{\bullet}):\quad\cdots\longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow{\rm Coker}(h^{-2-sm})\longrightarrow Z^{-sm}\longrightarrow Z^{-sm+1}\longrightarrow\cdots

is in
{\mathscr{K}}^{+}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
. Dually,
Z^{\bullet}
is isomorphic in
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
to a homotopy limit of
the canonical surjections:

\cdots\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{\geq-1-sm}(Z^{\bullet})\twoheadrightarrow\cdots\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{\geq-1-2s}(Z^{\bullet})\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{\leq-1-s}(Y^{\bullet})\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{\leq-1}(Y^{\bullet}).

We can show
Z^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}
. Hence
Q_{\rho}\circ Q(Z^{\bullet})\simeq Z^{\bullet}
and
J\circ J_{\rho}(Z^{\bullet})=0
.
Thus
\Psi^{\prime}(\underline{U})=0
and
U\in\mathscr{T}.

(2)
Suppose
\Lambda
has finite global dimension. Then
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})=0={\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
,
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})={\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
and
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})={\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{I}
. Hence
Q_{\lambda}\circ Q
and
Q_{\rho}\circ Q
are naturally isomorphic to Id. This implies that
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
are naturally isomorphic to Id, while
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
are zero functor.
\square

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
X
be an
A
-module. Then the following hold.

(1)
If
X\in M^{\bot>0}
, then
X\in\mathscr{S}
if and only if for any
A
-module
Y
,

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}_{X}),{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y}))=0
, where

\mu^{\bullet}_{X}:M^{\bullet}_{X}\to X
is an
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution of
X
.

(2)
If
X\in{{}^{\bot>0}}M
, then
\nu_{A}(X)\in\mathscr{T}
if and only if for any
A
-module
Y
,
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet}_{Y}),{\rm Con}(\sigma^{\bullet}_{X}))=0
where
\sigma^{\bullet}_{X}:X\to M^{\bullet}_{X}
is an
{\rm Add}(M)
-coresolution of
X
.

Proof.

(1)
Let
\pi^{\bullet}:=\pi^{\bullet}_{X}
,
\lambda^{\bullet}:=\lambda^{\bullet}_{X}
and
\mu^{\bullet}:=\mu^{\bullet}_{X}
. Then
S(X)={\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})
. Since
\mu^{\bullet}
is an
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution of
X
(see Definition
2.5
),
G(\mu^{\bullet}):G(M^{\bullet}_{X})\to G(X)
is a quasi-isomorphism; equivalently,

{\rm Con}(G(\mu^{\bullet}))
is exact. Moreover,
{\rm Con}(G(\mu^{\bullet}))=G({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}))
. As
{{}_{A}}M
is a generator,
{\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet})
is exact.
By
P^{\bullet}_{X}\in{\mathscr{K}}^{-}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
, the identity map of
X
can be lifted to a unique morphism
h^{\bullet}:P^{\bullet}_{X}\to M^{\bullet}_{X}
in
{\mathscr{K}}(A)
such that
h^{\bullet}\mu^{\bullet}=\pi^{\bullet}
. This implies
G(h^{\bullet})G(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})=G(\pi^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})
. By the octahedral axiom of triangulated category, there exists a triangle in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
:

(\ast):\;\;{\rm Con}(G(h^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow\mu\circ G\circ S(X)\longrightarrow{\rm Con}(G(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow{\rm Con}(G(h^{\bullet}))[1]

where
\mu\circ G\circ S(X)={\rm Con}(G(\pi^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))
. Since
X\in M^{\bot>0}
, the morphism
G(\lambda^{\bullet}):G(X)\to G(I^{\bullet}_{X})
is a quasi-isomorphism.
Thus
G(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})=G(\mu^{\bullet})G(\lambda^{\bullet})
is a composition of two quasi-isomorphisms. This means

{\rm Con}(G(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
.
It follows from
{\rm Con}(G(h^{\bullet}))\in\mathscr{K}^{-}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda)_{P}
that

Q_{\lambda}\circ Q(\mu\circ G\circ S(X))\simeq{\rm Con}(G(h^{\bullet}))\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;I\circ I_{\lambda}(\mu\circ G\circ S(X))\simeq{\rm Con}(G(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})).

Since the composition of
G
with
S_{e}
is isomorphic to the identity functor, we apply
S_{e}
to the triangle
(\ast)
and get another triangle
{\rm Con}(h^{\bullet})\to S(X)\to{\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})\to{\rm Con}(h^{\bullet})[1]
in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))
. Further, by applying the functor
\ell_{M}
(see Lemma
3.5
) to this triangle, we are led to a triangle in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
:

\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(h^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow\ell_{M}\circ S(X)\longrightarrow\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(h^{\bullet}))[1].

Clearly,
\ell_{M}\circ S(X)=S(X)
since
S(X)\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
. From
Z^{0}\circ S(X)\simeq X
, we obtain a triangle in

A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
:

Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(h^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(h^{\bullet}))[1].

Thus

\Phi(X)=Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(h^{\bullet}))
and
\Psi(X)=Z^{0}\circ\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})).

Note that
Z^{0}:{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
is an equivalence. Hence
\Psi(X)=0
(equivalently,
X\in\mathscr{S}
) if and only if
\ell_{M}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))=0
if and only if
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}),Q^{\bullet})=0
for any
Q^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
.

Since
{\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet})\in\mathscr{K}^{+}_{\rm ac}(A)
and
\mathscr{K}^{+}_{\rm ac}(A)\subseteq{{}^{\bot}}{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
,
there holds
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet}),Q^{\bullet}[n])=0
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Applying
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}(-,Q^{\bullet}[n])
to the triangle

{\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet})\to{\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet})\to{\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet})\to{\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet})[1]

in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(A)
, we get
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}),Q^{\bullet})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}),Q^{\bullet})
. Now, let
Y
be the kernel of the
0
-th differential of
Q^{\bullet}
. Taking the canonical truncation on
Q^{\bullet}
at degree
0
, we obtain a subcomplex
\tau_{\leq 0}Q^{\bullet}
of
Q^{\bullet}
, which is acyclic and isomorphic to
{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y})
in
{\mathscr{K}}(A)
. Since the inclusion
\tau_{\leq 0}Q^{\bullet}\subseteq Q^{\bullet}
induces
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}),\tau_{\leq 0}Q^{\bullet})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}),Q^{\bullet})
, it follows that
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}),{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y}))\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}),Q^{\bullet})
. So
X\in\mathscr{S}
if and only if

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}),{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y}))=0
. This shows
(1)
.

(2)
Let
\nu:=\nu_{A}
,
Z:=\nu(X)
and
\sigma^{\bullet}:=\sigma^{\bullet}_{X}
. Then

F\circ S(Z)=F\big({\rm Con}(\nu(\pi^{\bullet}\lambda^{\bullet}))\big)={\rm Con}\big(F(\nu\pi^{\bullet})F(\nu\lambda^{\bullet})\big).

Since
X\in{{}^{\bot>0}}M
and
\nu
is an auto-equivalence of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, there hold
D{\rm Tor}_{i}^{A}(\Lambda e,Z)\simeq{\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(Z,D(\Lambda e))\simeq{\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(Z,\nu M)\simeq{\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(X,M)=0
for any
i\geq 1
. Hence
F(\nu\pi^{\bullet})
is a quasi-isomorphism, that is,
{\rm Con}(F(\nu\pi^{\bullet}))
is exact. Thus
F\circ S(Z)\simeq{\rm Con}(F(\nu\lambda^{\bullet}))
in
{\mathscr{D}}(\Lambda)
, where
\nu\lambda^{\bullet}:Z\to\nu(I^{\bullet}_{X})
is an injective coresolution of
Z
. To calculate
Q_{\rho}\circ Q\circ\mu^{\prime}({\rm Con}(F(\nu\lambda^{\bullet})))
in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
, we show that
F(\nu\sigma^{\bullet}):F(Z)\to F\circ\nu(M^{\bullet}_{X})
is an injective coresolution of
F(Z)
, and then replace
F(Z)
by its deleted injective coresolution.

In fact, by the proof of Lemma
3.1
(3), the adjoint pair
(F,S_{e})
induces an equivalence
{\rm Add}(\nu M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}}
. This implies
F(\nu(M^{\bullet}_{X}))\in\mathscr{K}^{+}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
. It remains to show that

F(\nu\sigma^{\bullet})
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since
D:\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is exact and detects zero objects, we only need to show that
DF(\nu\sigma^{\bullet})
is a quasi-isomorphism. However, by Lemma
3.1
(2),
DF(\nu\sigma^{\bullet})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(\sigma^{\bullet},M)
which is a quasi-isomorphism by the construction of
\sigma
. Thus
F(\nu\sigma^{\bullet})
is an injective coresolution of
F(Z)
.

Now, let
f^{\bullet}:M^{\bullet}_{X}\to I^{\bullet}_{X}
be a chain map which lifts the identity map of
X
. Then there is a canonical triangle

{\rm Con}(F\circ\nu(\pi^{\bullet}\sigma^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow\mu^{\prime}\circ F\circ S(Z)\longrightarrow{\rm Con}(F\circ\nu(f^{\bullet}))\longrightarrow{\rm Con}(F\circ\nu(\pi^{\bullet}\sigma^{\bullet}))[1]

in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\mbox{\rm e-ac}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
, where the first term lies in
{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
and the third one lies in
\mathscr{K}^{+}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Inj}})
.
Thus

J\circ J_{\rho}\circ\mu^{\prime}\circ F\circ S(Z)\simeq{\rm Con}(F\circ\nu(f^{\bullet}))\quad\mbox{and}\quad Q_{\rho}\circ Q\circ\mu^{\prime}\circ F\circ S(Z)\simeq{\rm Con}(F\circ\nu(\pi^{\bullet}\sigma^{\bullet})).

Since the composition of
F
with
S_{e}
is isomorphic to the identity functor, it follows that

\Psi^{\prime}(Z)=Z^{0}\circ r_{M}({\rm Con}(\nu(\pi^{\bullet}\sigma^{\bullet})))\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Phi^{\prime}(Z)=Z^{0}\circ r_{M}({\rm Con}(\nu f^{\bullet})).

Dually, by the equivalence of
Z^{0}
and the inclusion
\mathscr{K}^{+}_{\rm ac}(A)\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}(A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})^{\bot}
,
we can show that
Z\in\mathscr{T}
if and only if
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet}_{U}),{\rm Con}(\nu\sigma^{\bullet}))=0
for any
A
-module
U
.
Since
\nu
is an auto-equivalence of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, we have
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet}_{U}),{\rm Con}(\nu\sigma^{\bullet}))\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet}_{U}),\nu({\rm Con}(\sigma^{\bullet})))\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\lambda^{\bullet}_{Y}),{\rm Con}(\sigma^{\bullet}))

with
Y:=\nu^{-}(U)
. Thus
(2)
holds.
\square

The following result is a consequence of Lemma
3.7
(1) and Proposition
3.8
.

Corollary 3.9
.

If
{{}_{A}}M
is self-orthogonal or
\Omega
-periodic, then
M\in\mathscr{S}
and
\nu_{A}(M)\in\mathscr{T}
.

3.3
Recollements of stable module categories induced by self-orthogonal modules

In this subsection, we apply the triangle endofunctors in Section
3.2
to construct a recollement of the stable module category of a self-injective algebra from a generator with conditions that are satisfied for a self-orthogonal or an
\Omega
-periodic generator.

As is known,
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
is compactly generated and the inclusion of
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\to A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
induces a triangle equivalence from
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{mod}}}}
to the full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
consisting of all compact objects.
Since
{{}_{A}}M
is finitely generated,
M
is compact in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.
For a set
\Delta
of integers, let

\underline{M}^{\bot\Delta}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X[n])=0\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;n\in\Delta\},

{{}^{\bot\Delta}}\underline{M}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M[n])=0\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;n\in\Delta\}.

For simplicity, we write
\underline{M}^{\bot}
and
{{}^{\bot}}\underline{M}
for
\underline{M}^{\bot\mathbb{Z}}
and
{{}^{\bot\mathbb{Z}}}\underline{M}
, respectively.
Then
\underline{M}^{\bot}
is a full triangulated subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
closed under direct sums and direct products.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
X
be an
A
-module.

(1)

X\in\underline{M}^{\bot}
if and only if
G\circ S(X)
(equivalently,
F\circ S(X)
) is an exact complex.

(2)
If
X\in\underline{M}^{\bot}
, then
\Phi(X)=0
and
\Phi^{\prime}(X)=0
.

Proof.
By Lemma
3.1
(1),
F
and
G
are naturally isomorphic on
A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
. Thus
G\circ S(X)\simeq F\circ S(X)
as complexes. So we show (1) for
G\circ S(X)
. Let
0\to X_{1}\to P\to X_{0}\to 0
be an exact sequence of
A
-modules with
P\in A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
.
Then
0\to G(X_{1})\to G(P)\to G(X_{0})\to 0
is exact if and only if
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X_{0})=0
.
This implies
(1)
. Moreover,
(2)
follows from
(1)
and the definitions of
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
.
\square

We need the following result which is concluded from the Auslander-Reiten formula (see
[
6
]
).

Lemma 3.11
.

If
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, then there is a natural isomorphism

D{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,-)\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(-,\nu_{A}(X)[-1]):\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow\underline{{\rm End}}_{A}(X)^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}.

Proof.
Since
X
is finitely generated, it follows from
[
6
, Proposition 2.2]
that

D{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,-)\simeq{\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(-,D{\rm Tr}(X)):\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow\underline{{\rm End}}_{A}(X)^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}

where
D{\rm Tr}
is the Auslander-Reiten translation on
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{mod}}}}
. Since
A
is self-injective,

D{\rm Tr}\simeq\Omega^{2}_{A}\circ\nu_{A}
as functors on
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{mod}}}}
. This implies that

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(-,D{\rm Tr}(X))&\simeq{\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(-,\Omega^{2}_{A}\circ\nu_{A}(X))\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(\Omega_{A}(-),\Omega^{2}_{A}\circ\nu_{A}(X))\\
&\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(-,\Omega_{A}\circ\nu_{A}(X))={\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(-,\nu_{A}(X)[-1])\end{array}

on
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. Thus Lemma
3.11
holds.

\square

To construct recollements of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
from compact objects, we establish a result on torsion pairs.

Lemma 3.12
.

(1)

\big({\rm Loc}(\underline{M}),\,\underline{M}^{\bot}\big)
and
\big(\underline{M}^{\bot},{\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})\big)
are hereditary torsion pairs in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

(2)
If
{{}_{A}}M\in\mathscr{S}
, then
\underline{\mathscr{S}}={\rm Im}(\Phi)={\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
and
{\rm Im}(\Psi)=\underline{M}^{\bot}
.

(3)
If
\nu_{A}(M)\in\mathscr{T}
, then
\underline{\mathscr{T}}={\rm Im}(\Phi^{\prime})={\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})
and
{\rm Im}(\Psi^{\prime})=\underline{M}^{\bot}
.

Proof.
(1) Since
{{}_{A}}M
is finitely generated, we have
\underline{M}^{\bot}={{}^{\bot}}\underline{\nu_{A}(M)}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
by Lemma
3.11
.
Clearly,
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
is compactly generated by simple modules because each
A
-module has a radical series of length less than or equal the Loewy length of
A
. Thus
(1)
holds by Proposition
2.3
.

(2)
Let
\mathscr{X}:={\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
and
\mathscr{Y}:=\underline{M}^{\bot}
. Suppose
M\in\mathscr{S}
. Then
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{S}}
because
\underline{\mathscr{S}}
is a full triangulated subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
closed under direct sums. By Lemma
3.10
(2),
\mathscr{Y}\subseteq{\rm Ker}(\Phi)
.

Let
N\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. By
(1)
, up to isomorphism, there is a unique triangle

X_{N}\to N\to Y^{N}\to X_{N}[1]
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
such that
X_{N}\in\mathscr{X}
and
Y^{N}\in\mathscr{Y}
.
This yields
\Psi(X_{N})=0=\Phi(Y^{N})
. Now, we apply Proposition
3.6
to the triangle and obtain the commutative diagram in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
:

\textstyle{\Phi(X_{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{X_{N}}}
\textstyle{\Phi(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{N}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(X_{N})[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{X_{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\eta}_{N}}
\textstyle{Y^{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\eta}_{Y^{N}}}
\textstyle{X_{N}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Psi(Y^{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi(X_{N})[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(X_{N})[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Phi(N)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(X_{N})[2]}

where all rows and columns are triangles. Thus
\Phi(N)\simeq X_{N}\in\mathscr{X}
and
\Psi(N)\simeq Y^{N}\in\mathscr{Y}
.
This implies
{\rm Im}(\Phi)\subseteq\mathscr{X}
and
{\rm Im}(\Psi)\subseteq\mathscr{Y}
. The inclusions in other direction follows from Proposition
3.6
. Thus
{\rm Im}(\Phi)=\mathscr{X}
and
{\rm Im}(\Psi)=\mathscr{Y}
. Since
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{S}}\subseteq{\rm Im}(\Phi)
and
\mathscr{Y}\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{T}}\subseteq{\rm Im}(\Phi)
, we have
\underline{\mathscr{S}}={\rm Im}(\Phi)
and
{\rm Im}(\Psi)=\underline{M}^{\bot}
.

(3) Similar, we can show
(3)
by Lemma
3.10
(2) and the pair
\big(\underline{M}^{\bot},{\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})\big)
in
(1)
.
\square

A consequence of Lemma
3.12
and Corollary
3.9
is the following.

Corollary 3.13
.

Suppose that
{{}_{A}}M
is self-orthogonal or
\Omega
-periodic. Then
\mathscr{S}
is the smallest thick subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
containing
M
and being closed under direct sums, while
\mathscr{T}
is the smallest thick subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
containing
\nu_{A}(M)
and being closed under direct products.

Now, we are in position to prove the following main result of this section.

Theorem 3.14
.

Suppose
A
is a self-injective Artin algebra and
{}_{A}M
is generator in
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. If
{{}_{A}}M\in\mathscr{S}
and
\nu_{A}(M)\in\mathscr{T}
, then there exists a recollement of triangulated categories:

\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}
\textstyle{A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi}}
\textstyle{{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}

such that

\Phi={\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Phi},\quad\Psi={\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Psi},\quad\Psi^{\prime}={\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\Phi^{\prime\prime}=\Phi^{\prime}\circ{\rm inc}.

Moreover, the functor
\Phi^{\prime\prime}
restricts to a triangle equivalence
{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)}).

Proof.
Suppose
{{}_{A}}M\in\mathscr{S}
. By Lemma
3.12
(2), we have the factorisation of
\Phi
and
\Psi
:

\Phi:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\Phi}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})\hookrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Psi:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\Psi}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{M}^{\bot}\hookrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}.

By Lemma
3.12
(1),
\big({\rm Loc}(\underline{M}),\,\underline{M}^{\bot}\big)
is a hereditary torsion pair in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. Then the proof of Lemma
3.12
(2) together with
[
13
, Chapter I, Prop. 2.3]
implies that
\widetilde{\Phi}
is a right adjoint of the inclusion
{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
and that
\widetilde{\Psi}
is a left adjoint of the inclusion
\underline{M}^{\bot}\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

Suppose
\nu_{A}(M)\in\mathscr{T}
. Dually, from the torsion pair
\big(\underline{M}^{\bot},{\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})\big)
in Lemma
3.12
(1) and from Lemma
3.12
(3), we obtain the factorisations of
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Psi^{\prime}
:

\Phi^{\prime}:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\Phi^{\prime}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})\hookrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Psi^{\prime}:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{M}^{\bot}\hookrightarrow A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}

such that
\widetilde{\Phi^{\prime}}
is a left adjoint of the inclusion
{\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
and

\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}
is a right adjoint of the inclusion
\underline{M}^{\bot}\to A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. Recall that there is a correspondence between TTF (torsion-torsionfree) triples and recollements of triangulated categories (see, for example,
[
13
, Chapter I. 2]
or
[
17
, Section 2.3]
). Thus
Theorem
3.14
follows from Lemma
3.12
(1) and
[
17
, Lemma 2.6]
.
\square

Combining Theorem
3.14
with Corollary
3.9
, we obtain the corollary.

Corollary 3.15
.

Let
A
be a self-injective algebra.
If
{{}_{A}}M
is self-orthogonal or
\Omega
-periodic, then there exists a recollement of triangulated categories:

\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}
\textstyle{A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi}}
\textstyle{{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}

in which the functors are the same as the ones in Theorem
3.14
.

Later we will see that the above recollement restricts to the one of relative stable categories.

3.4
Categories of Gorenstein-projective modules

In the subsection we describe the category of Gorenstein-projective modules over the endomorphism
algebra of a self-orthogonal generator.

Recall that the
M
-stable category

A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}/[M]
of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is defined to be the quotient category of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
modulo
{\rm Add}(M)
. From now on, we set

\mathscr{D}:=A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}/[M]\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{M}(X_{1},X_{2}):={\rm Hom}_{\mathscr{D}}(X_{1},X_{2})

for
X_{1},X_{2}\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. We say that
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
are
M
-stably isomorphic
if they are isomorphic in
\mathscr{D}
. For a full subcategory
\mathscr{U}
of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, we denote by
\mathscr{U}/[M]
the full subcategory of
\mathscr{D}
consisting of all objects
X
which are
M
-stably isomorphic to objects of
\mathscr{U}
.

As
M
is finitely generated and
{\rm Add}(M)={\rm Prod}(M)
, we know that
{\rm Add}(M)
is a functorially finite subcategory in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.
According to
[
13
, Chap. II. 1]
,
\mathscr{D}
is a
pretriangulated category
mainly consisting of the following data:

(1)
An adjoint pair
(\Omega_{M}^{-},\Omega_{M})
of additive endofunctors
\Omega_{M}^{-},\Omega_{M}:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}
.

For an
A
-module
X
,
\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)
is defined to be the cokernel of a minimal left
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation
\ell_{X}:X\to M^{X}
of
X
, while

\Omega_{M}(X)
is defined to be the kernel of a minimal right
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation
r_{X}:M_{X}\to X
of
X
. The existence of minimal approximations
follows from Remark
3.2
. Moreover,
\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)
and
\Omega_{M}(X)
are unique up to isomorphism.

(2)
A collection of
right triangles
(up to isomorphism) of the form

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)

arising from an exact commutative diagram in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{X}}
\textstyle{M^{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where
{\rm Hom}_{A}(f,M):{\rm Hom}_{A}(Y,M)\to{\rm Hom}_{A}(X,M)
is surjective.

(3)
A collection of
left triangles
(up to isomorphism) of the form
\Omega_{M}(Z)\to X\to Y\to Z
which is defined in a dual way as in
(2)
.

(4)
Right triangles (respectively, left triangles) satisfy all the axioms of a triangulated category, except that
\Omega_{M}^{-}
(respectively,

\Omega_{M}
) is not necessarily an equivalence.

In the following,
\Omega_{M}
is called the
M
-syzygy
functor on
\mathscr{D}
. For an
A
-module
X
, we put
\Omega_{M}^{0}(X):=X
, and
\Omega_{M}^{n}(X):=\Omega_{M}(\Omega_{M}^{n-1}(X))
for
n\geq 1
. The functor
\Omega_{M}^{n}
is called the
n
-th
M
-syzygy
functor on
\mathscr{D}
. Dually,
\Omega_{M}^{-}
is called the
M
-cosyzygy
functor and the
n
-th
M
-cosyzygy
functor
\Omega_{M}^{-n}
is defined dually. If
M
is a self-orthogonal generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, then
(\Omega_{M}^{-n},\Omega_{M}^{n}):\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}
is an adjoint pair for
n\geq 2
, see
[
16
, Lemmas 3.2 and 3.3]
.

The following simple observation will be used in the later discussions.

Lemma 3.16
.

(1)
Let
X
and
Y
be
A
-modules. Then
X
and
Y
are
M
-stably isomorphic if and only if there are
M_{1},M_{2}\in{\rm Add}(M)
such that
X\oplus M_{1}\simeq Y\oplus M_{2}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.

(2)
Let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}Z\rightarrow X[1]
be a triangle in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. If
Z\in{{}^{\bot 1}}M
, then there is a right
triangle
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}Z\rightarrow\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)
in
\mathscr{D}
.

Proof.

(1)
This can be proved similarly as done in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

(2)
Up to isomorphism of triangles in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, we can assume that the sequence
0\to X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}Z\to 0
is exact. If
Z\in{{}^{\bot 1}}M
, then
{\rm Hom}_{A}(f,M)
is surjective. This implies
(2)
.
\square

By Lemma
3.16
, if
\mathscr{U}
is a full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
containing
{\rm Add}(M)
and being closed under both direct summands and finite direct sums, then
\mathscr{U}/[M]
is closed under direct summands in
\mathscr{D}
.

Let

\mathscr{G}:=M^{\bot>0}\cap{{}^{\bot>0}}M,\quad\mathscr{C}:=\mathscr{G}/[M],\quad\mathscr{G}_{0}:=\mathscr{G}\cap A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}.

Then
\mathscr{G}
is always closed under filtered colimits in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, that is, colimits of filtered diagrams
I\to A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
with
I
an essentially small, filtered category.

In fact, since
M
is finitely generated, the functor
{\rm Ext}_{A}^{n}(M,-)
commutes with filtered colimits for each
n\geq 0
.
Then
M^{\bot>0}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is closed under filtered colimits. Thanks to
M\simeq D(DM)
as
A
-modules,

M
is pure-injective. It follows that
{\rm Ext}_{A}^{n}(-,M)
sends filtered colimits to filtered limits, and therefore

{{}^{\bot>0}}M\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is also closed under filtered colimits. Thus
\mathscr{G}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is closed under filtered colimits.

Let
\lim\limits_{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{G}_{0}
denote the full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
consisting of all filtered colimits of modules in
\mathscr{G}_{0}
. Then
\lim\limits_{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{G}_{0}\subseteq\mathscr{G}
.

In the rest of this subsection, we assume that
{{}_{A}}M
is
self-orthogonal
.

The following result is a unbounded version of
[
16
, Lemma 3.5]
.

Lemma 3.17
.

(1)
The category
\mathscr{G}
(respectively,
\mathscr{G}_{0}
) is a Frobenius category with the shift functor given by
\Omega_{M}^{-}
. The full subcategory of projective-injective objects of
\mathscr{G}
(respectively,
\mathscr{G}_{0}
) equals
{\rm Add}(M)
(respectively,
{\rm add}(M)
). In particular,
\mathscr{G}/[M]
is a triangulated category.

(2)
The functor
G:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
restricts to equivalences of Frobenius categories:

\mathscr{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}\quad\mbox{\rm and}\quad\mathscr{G}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Gproj}}.

In particular, there are equivalences of triangulated categories:

\mathscr{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{-}\underline{\rm GProj}\quad\mbox{\rm and}\quad\mathscr{G}_{0}/[M]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{-}\underline{\rm Gproj}.

Proof.
For
n\geq 1
, we define

\mathscr{G}_{n}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\mid{\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(M,\Omega_{M}^{-j}(X))=0={\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(\Omega_{M}^{j}(X),M)\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;j\geq 0\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;1\leq i\leq n\}

(see also
[
16
, Definition 3.4]
). Then
\mathscr{G}_{n}
is a Frobenius category and there is a chain of full subcategories of
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
:

\mathscr{G}_{1}\supseteq\mathscr{G}_{2}\supseteq\cdots\supseteq\mathscr{G}_{n}\supseteq\mathscr{G}_{n+1}\supseteq\cdots.

Moreover,
G
restricts to an equivalence of Frobenius categories:
\mathscr{G}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Gproj}}
for
n\geq 1
.
It follows that the inclusions
\mathscr{G}_{n+1}\to\mathscr{G}_{n}
are equivalences of additive categories. Since
\mathscr{G}_{n}
are closed under isomorphisms in
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, we have
\mathscr{G}_{n}=\mathscr{G}_{n+1}
for all
n\geq 1
. As
M
is self-orthogonal,
\mathscr{G}_{0}
is closed under taking
\Omega_{M}
and
\Omega^{-}_{M}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. Consequently,
\mathscr{G}_{0}\subseteq\bigcap_{n\geq 1}^{\infty}\mathscr{G}_{n}\subseteq\mathscr{G}_{0}
. This implies
\mathscr{G}_{0}=\mathscr{G}_{n}
, and thus Lemma
3.17
holds for
\mathscr{G}_{0}
.

Note that
G
commutes with direct sums and restricts to an equivalence
{\rm Add}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
.
As done in the proof of
[
16
, Lemma 3.5]
, we can show that Lemma
3.17
holds first for

\mathscr{\bf{G}}_{n}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\mid{\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(M,\Omega_{M}^{-j}(X))=0={\rm Ext}_{A}^{i}(\Omega_{M}^{j}(X),M)\;\;\mbox{for }\;\;j\geq 0\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;1\leq i\leq n\}

and then for
\mathscr{G}
.
\square

Consequently, we get a characterization of virtually Gorenstein algebras in terms of compact objects.

Proposition 3.18
.

Let
A
be a self-injective algebra and
M
a self-orthogonal generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. Then
{\rm End}_{A}(M)
is virtually Gorenstein if and only if
\mathscr{G}=\lim\limits_{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{G}_{0}
if and only if each compact object of
\mathscr{C}
is
M
-stably isomorphic to a finitely generated module.

Proof.
By
[
14
, Theorem 5]
and
[
11
, Theorem 8.2]
,
\Lambda:={\rm End}_{A}(M)
is virtually Gorenstein if and only if
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}=\lim\limits_{\longrightarrow}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Gproj}})
if and only if each compact object of
\Lambda\mbox{-}\underline{\rm GProj}
is isomorphic to an object of
\Lambda\mbox{-}\underline{\rm Gproj}
. Note that
G:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is fully faithful and commutes with filtered colimits. Moreover, since
{{}_{A}}M
is a generator, two
A
-modules
X
and
Y
are isomorphic if and only if
G(X)
and
G(Y)
are isomorphic. Now the equivalences in Proposition
3.18
follow from Lemmas
3.17
and
3.16
.
\square

A complex
P^{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
is called
totally acyclic
if both
P^{\bullet}
and
{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\Lambda}(P^{\bullet},\Lambda)
are acyclic. Let
\mathscr{K}_{\rm tac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
be the full subcategory of
{\mathscr{K}}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
consisting of totally acyclic complexes. It is known that there is a triangle equivalence
\Lambda\mbox{-}\underline{\rm GProj}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{K}_{\rm tac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}})
which sends a Gorenstein-projective
\Lambda
-module to its complete projective resolution. Composing this equivalence with the equivalence

\mathscr{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{-}\underline{\rm GProj}
in Lemma
3.17
(2), we obtain a triangle equivalence

{\rm Hom}_{A}(M,M^{\bullet}_{-}):\;\;\mathscr{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{K}_{\rm tac}(\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}),\;\;X\mapsto{\rm Hom}_{A}(M,M^{\bullet}_{X})

where
M^{\bullet}_{X}:=(M_{X}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{\rm ac}({\rm Add}(M))
is defined by concatenating an
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution
\cdots\to M_{X}^{-2}\to M_{X}^{-1}\to M_{X}^{0}\to X\to 0
of
X
with an
{\rm Add}(M)
-coresolution
0\to X\to M_{X}^{1}\to M_{X}^{2}\to M_{X}^{3}\to\cdots
of
X
at the position
X
. The complex
M^{\bullet}_{X}
is called a
complete
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution
of
X
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is an additive functor

\Psi_{n}:=H^{n}\big({\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,M^{\bullet}_{-})\big):\;\;\mathscr{C}\longrightarrow\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-Mod}},\;\;X\mapsto H^{n}\big({\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,M^{\bullet}_{X})\big)

which is
homological
in the sense that applying
\Psi_{n}
to every triangle
X_{1}\to X_{2}\to X_{3}\to\Omega_{M}^{-}(X_{1})
in
\mathscr{C}
yields an exact sequence

\cdots\to\Psi_{n}(X_{1})\to\Psi_{n}(X_{2})\to\Psi_{n}(X_{3})\to\Psi_{n+1}(X_{1})\to\cdots.

Remark 3.19
.

By Lemma
3.17
(1) and Remark
3.2
(2), we have the following observation.

For each
X\in\mathscr{G}
, there are isomorphisms
X\simeq\Omega_{M}^{-}\Omega_{M}(X)\oplus M_{0}\simeq\Omega_{M}\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)\oplus M_{1}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}

with
M_{0},M_{1}\in{\rm Add}(M)
such that
\Omega_{M}^{-}\Omega_{M}(X)
and
\Omega_{M}\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)
have no direct summands in
{\rm Add}(M)
.
Thus, if
X\in\mathscr{G}
has no direct summands in
{\rm Add}(M)
, then we can choose
the
n
-th differential
d_{X}^{n}:M_{X}^{n}\to M_{X}^{n+1}
such that the induced map
M_{X}^{n}\to{\rm Im}(d_{X}^{n})
is a minimal right
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation of
{\rm Im}(d_{X}^{n})
.

Lemma 3.20
.

(1)
The functor
F\circ\nu_{A}:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
restricts to an equivalence:

\mathscr{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
of Frobenious categories.

(2)

(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1})
and
({{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G},\mathscr{G})
are cotorsion pairs in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
such that
\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}={\rm Add}(M)={{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}
. In particular,
\mathscr{G}
is functorially finite in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.

(3)
The inclusion
\mathscr{C}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}
admits a left adjoint
T:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
which is induced from minimal left
\mathscr{G}
-approximations of modules. Moreover,
T
preserves compact objects, sends right triangles to triangles and
commutes with the functor
\Omega_{M}^{-}
.

(4)
The set
\{T(X)\mid X\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\}
is a compact generating set of
\mathscr{C}
.

Proof.

(1)
Since the adjoint pair
(\nu_{\Lambda},\nu_{\Lambda}^{-})
induces quasi-inverse equivalences

\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
,
(1)
follows from Lemmas
3.17
(2) and
3.1
(2) together with Remark
3.2
(1).

(2)
Since
\Omega^{-}_{M}(\mathscr{G})\subseteq\mathscr{G}
by Lemma
3.17
(1), the sequence
0\to X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ell_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}M^{X}\to\Omega^{-}_{M}(X)\to 0
splits whenever
X\in\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}
. This implies

\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}\subseteq{\rm Add}(M)
. Clearly,
{\rm Add}(M)\subseteq\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}
. Thus
\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}={\rm Add}(M)
. Dually,
{{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}={\rm Add}(M)
.

Next, we show that
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1})
is a cotorsion pair in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
.
Since
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is an abelian category with enough projectives and injectives, it suffices to show that, for any
A
-module
X
, there is an exact sequence
0\to X_{2}\to X_{1}\to X\to 0
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
such that
X_{1}\in\mathscr{G}
and
X_{2}\in\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}
(for example, see
[
13
, Lemma V. 3.3]
).

Since each
\Lambda
-module admits a minimal right
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}
-approximation, we take a minimal right
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GProj}}
-approximation of
G(X)
, say
g:Y\to G(X)
. By Lemma
3.17
(2), we can assume
Y=G(X_{1})
for some
X_{1}\in\mathscr{G}
. As
G
is fully faithful and
M
is a generator, there is a surjective map

f:X_{1}\to X
of
A
-modules such that
g=G(f)
and
f
is a minimal right
\mathscr{G}
-approximation of
X
. Since
\mathscr{G}
is closed under extensions in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, it follows from Wakamatsu’s Lemma that
{\rm Ker}(f)\in\mathscr{G}^{\bot 1}
. Hence the sequence
0\to{\rm Ker}(f)\to X_{1}\to X\to 0
is a desired one.

Similarly, to show that
({{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G},\mathscr{G})
is a cotorsion pair in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, it is enough to prove that there is an exact sequence
0\to X\to T^{X}\to C^{X}\to 0
of
A
-modules such that
T^{X}\in\mathscr{G}
and
C^{X}\in{{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G}
.

Let
\overline{F}=F\circ\nu_{A}:A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}\to\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Then
\overline{F}
is fully faithful and restricts to an equivalence
\mathscr{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
by
(1)
. Since each
\Lambda
-module admits a minimal left
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
-approximation,
there is a map
h^{X}:X\to T^{X}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
with
T^{X}\in\mathscr{G}
such that
\overline{F}(h^{X})
is a minimal left
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-GInj}}
-approximation of
\overline{F}(X)
. This implies that
h^{X}
is a minimal left
\mathscr{G}
-approximation of
X
. Moreover,
h^{X}
is injective. This is due to
D(A)\in\mathscr{G}
and
{\rm Coker}(h^{X})\in{{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G}
by the dual of Wakamatsu’s Lemma. Thus the sequence

0\to X\to T^{X}\to{\rm Coker}(h^{X})\to 0
is the one as desired.

(3)
Since
({{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G},\mathscr{G})
is a cotorsion pair with
{{}^{\bot 1}}\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{G}={\rm Add}(M)
by (2), the functor
T:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
exists (see the comments after Definition
2.4
).
As the inclusion functor
\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
preserves
direct sums, we know that
T
preserves compact objects. It is known that
\mathscr{C}
is a triangulated category,
\mathscr{D}
is a pretriangulated category and

\mathscr{C}
is a pretriangulated subcategory of
\mathscr{D}
. Thus the last assertion follows from the dual version of
[
13
, Proposition II. 2.6]
.

(4)
We show that
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}/[M]
is a compact generating set of
\mathscr{D}
.
Clearly, if
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, then
X
is compact in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
, and also compact in
\mathscr{D}
by Lemma
2.1
(2). Let
Y\in A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
such that
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{M}(X,Y)=0
for all
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. Then each map from
X
to
Y
factorizes through an object of
{\rm Add}(M)
, and particularly, through
M_{Y}
via the minimal right
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation
r_{Y}:M_{Y}\to Y
of
Y
.
Recall that, for an Artin algebra
B
, an exact sequence
0\to X_{1}\rightarrow X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}X_{3}\to 0
of
B
-modules is called
pure-exact
if
{\rm Hom}_{B}(Z,g)
is surjective for any
Z\in B\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
; equivalently,
0\to L\otimes_{B}X_{1}\to L\otimes_{B}X_{2}\to L\otimes_{B}X_{3}\to 0
is exact for any
L\in B^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. This implies that
0\to\Omega_{M}(Y)\to M_{Y}\to Y\to 0
is pure-exact in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Note that there is a natural isomorphism
{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(U,G(-))\simeq{\rm Hom}_{A}(M\otimes_{\Lambda}U,-)
for any
\Lambda
-module
U
and that
{{}_{A}}M\otimes_{\Lambda}U\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
if
U\in\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
. Hence
0\to G(\Omega_{M}(Y))\to G(M_{Y})\to G(Y)\to 0
is pure-exact in
\Lambda\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Since
G(M_{Y})
is projective, there holds
{\rm Tor}^{\Lambda}_{1}(V,G(Y))=0
for all
V\in\Lambda^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Thus
G(Y)
is flat, and therefore projective since
\Lambda
is an Artin algebra. It follows that
G(Y)
is a direct summand of
G(M_{Y})
. Then
Y\in{\rm Add}(M)
due to
A\in{\rm add}({{}_{A}}M)
. Hence
Y=0
in
\mathscr{D}
and
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}/[M]
is a compact generating set of
\mathscr{D}
.

By
(3)
, if
X\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, then
T(X)
is compact in
\mathscr{C}
. Moreover, since
T
is a left adjoint of the inclusion
\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
,
one can check that
T
always preserves generating sets. This shows
(4)
.
\square

4
Restrictions of recollements to relative stable categories

In this section we prove Theorem
1.5
. As a preparation of the proof, we first show that the recollement in Corollary
3.15
restricts to a recollement of
\mathscr{C}
.
Throughout this section we set up the following.

Assumption:

Let
A
be a self-injective algebra and
{{}_{A}}M
a self-orthogonal and Nakayama-stable generator for
A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
.

We set
\Gamma:={\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(M)
.
By Lemma
3.11
, there exists a natural isomorphism of additive functors:

(\lozenge)\quad D{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,-)\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(-,\nu_{A}(M)[-1]):\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}.

We define the following categories related to
M
:

\mathscr{Y}:=\{Y\in A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,Y[n]=0\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;n\leq 0\},

\mathscr{X}:=\{X\in A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,Y)=0\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;Y\in\mathscr{Y}\},

\mathscr{H}:=\mathscr{X}\cap\mathscr{Y}[1].

Then
\mathscr{X}
is the smallest full subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
containing
M
and being closed under
[1]
, extensions and direct sums,
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
is a torsion pair in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, and
\mathscr{H}
is an abelian category and called the
heart
of
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
(see
[
10
]
). Clearly,
\underline{M}^{\bot}\subseteq\mathscr{Y}
and
\mathscr{X}\subseteq{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
. In general,
\mathscr{X}
has not to be a triangulated subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
since it is not necessarily closed under
[-1]
.

Proposition 4.1
.

\mathscr{X}=\underline{M}^{\bot>0}\cap\underline{\mathscr{S}}\,
,
\mathscr{Y}={{}^{\bot\geq-1}}\underline{M}
and
\mathscr{H}=\underline{M}^{\bot\neq 0}\cap\underline{\mathscr{S}}
.

Proof.
Since
{{}_{A}}M
is self-orthogonal, it follows from Lemma
3.12
(2) that
\underline{\mathscr{S}}={\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
. As
\underline{M}^{\bot>0}

contains
M
and is closed under
[1]
, extensions and direct sums in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, we have
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\underline{M}^{\bot>0}
.
Thanks to
\mathscr{X}\subseteq{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
, we obtain
\mathscr{X}\subseteq\underline{M}^{\bot>0}\cap{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
. To show the converse inclusion, we pick up
X\in\underline{M}^{\bot>0}\cap{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
and show
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,Y)=0
for any
Y\in\mathscr{Y}
.

Actually, it follows from
\underline{M}^{\bot>0}=\underline{M^{\bot>0}}
that
X\in M^{\bot>0}
. Note that
X
lies in
\mathscr{S}
. Let
\mu^{\bullet}_{X}:M^{\bullet}_{X}\to X
be an
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution of
X
. It follows from Proposition
3.8
(1) that
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}_{X}),{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y}))=0
for any
Y\in\mathscr{Y}
. Since
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,\Omega_{A}^{n}(Y))={\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,Y[-n])=0
for any
n\geq 0
, the chain map
\pi^{\bullet}_{Y}:P^{\bullet}_{Y}\to Y
is an
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution of
Y
. Thus each homomorphism
f:X\to Y
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
can be lifted to a chain map
f^{\bullet}:{\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}_{X})\to{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y})
in
{\mathscr{K}}(A)
. From
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{K}}(A)}({\rm Con}(\mu^{\bullet}_{X}),{\rm Con}(\pi^{\bullet}_{Y}))=0
, we see that
f
factorizes through the projective module
P_{Y}^{0}
. Thus
f=0
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
and
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,Y)=0
.

By definition,
\mathscr{Y}=\underline{M}^{\bot\leq 0}
. It follows from
{\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))={\rm add}({{}_{A}}M)
and
(\lozenge)
that
\mathscr{Y}={{}^{\bot\geq-1}}\underline{M}
. Since
\mathscr{H}=\mathscr{X}\cap\mathscr{Y}[1]
and
\mathscr{Y}[1]=\underline{M}^{\bot<0}
, one gets

\mathscr{H}=\underline{M}^{\bot\neq 0}\cap{\rm Loc}(\underline{M})=\underline{M}^{\bot\neq 0}\cap\underline{\mathscr{S}}
.
\square

4.1
Restrictions of recollements

In this subsection we consider the restriction of the recollement in Corollary
3.15

to relative stable categories. This leads to a part of the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

We begin with the following preparation.

Lemma 4.2
.

(1)

\underline{M}^{\bot}={{}^{\bot}}\underline{M},\quad\underline{\mathscr{G}}=\underline{M}^{\bot\neq 0,-1}={{}^{\bot\neq 0,-1}}\underline{M}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathscr{H}\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}\subseteq\mathscr{X}.

(2)
Let
\pi:\underline{\mathscr{G}}\to\mathscr{C}
be the canonical quotient functor. Then the following are true.

(a)
The composition
\underline{M}^{\bot}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathscr{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{C}
is a fully faithful triangle functor.
In particular, the image of this composition is a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
.

(b)
The composition
\mathscr{H}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
is a fully faithful additive functor.

Proof.
(1) Since
{\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))={\rm add}({{}_{A}}M)
,
(1)
follows from
(\lozenge)
and Proposition
4.1
.

(2) If
X\in\underline{M}^{\bot}
, then
X\in{{}^{\bot}}\underline{M}
by
(1)
. In this case,
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X)=0={\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M)
, and therefore
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M_{0},X)=0={\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M_{0})
for
M_{0}\in{\rm Add}(M)
. This implies that
\Omega_{A}(X)\simeq\Omega_{M}(X)
,
\Omega_{A}^{-}(X)\simeq\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)
in
\mathscr{D}
and
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,X^{\prime})=\underline{{\rm Hom}}_{M}(X,X^{\prime})
for any
A
-module
X^{\prime}
. Thus the composition in
(a)
is fully faithful. It is a triangle functor since
\underline{M}^{\bot}
is a full triangulated subcategory of
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. This shows
(a)
.

Now, let
X\in\mathscr{H}
. Then
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X[-1])=0
. Since
D{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X[-1])\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,\nu_{A}(M))
by
(\lozenge)
and
{\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))={\rm add}(M)
, we have
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M)=0
. It then follows from
{\rm Add}(\underline{M})={\rm Prod}(\underline{M})
that
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M^{\prime})=0
for
M^{\prime}\in{\rm Add}(M)
. Thus
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,Y)={\rm\underline{Hom}}_{M}(X,Y)
for any
A
-module
Y
. This implies
(b)
.
\square

Proposition 4.3
.

The recollement in Corollary
3.15
induces a recollement of triangulated categories:

\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi\circ{\rm inc}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi}}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}

Proof.
We first show that the recollement in Corollary
3.15
can be restricted to a “recollement” of additive categories with six additive functors:

(\sharp)\quad\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 54.0179pt\raise 6.16234pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

which satisfy the conditions
(1)
-
(3)
in Definition
2.2
. Obviously,
{\rm inc}:\underline{M}^{\bot}\to\underline{\mathscr{G}}
has left and right adjoints which are the restriction of the functors
\widetilde{\Psi}
and
\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}
in Corollary
3.15
to
\underline{\mathscr{G}}
, respectively. Now, we claim that
\widetilde{\Phi}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}
and
\Phi^{\prime\prime}(\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}})\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{G}}
. Then
({\rm inc},\widetilde{\Phi})
and
(\widetilde{\Phi},\Phi^{\prime\prime})
in
(\sharp)
are adjoint pairs.

By Corollary
3.13
,
\underline{\mathscr{S}}={\rm Loc}(\underline{M})
. By Theorem
3.14
, each
A
-module
X
is endowed with a triangle
\Psi(X)[-1]\to\Phi(X)\to X\to\Psi(X)
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
such that
\Phi(X)\in\underline{\mathscr{S}}
and
\Psi(X)\in\underline{M}^{\bot}
. Note that
\underline{\mathscr{G}}
contains
\underline{M}^{\bot}
and is closed under extensions of triangles in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, due to Lemma
4.2
. Since
\Psi(X)
and
\Psi(X)[-1]
lie in
\underline{M}^{\bot}
, we see that
X\in\underline{\mathscr{G}}
if and only if
\Phi(X)\in\underline{\mathscr{G}}
.
Thus
\widetilde{\Phi}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})=\Phi(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}
. Similarly, by the triangle

\Psi^{\prime}(X)\to X\to\Phi^{\prime}(X)\to\Psi^{\prime}(X)[1]
in Proposition
3.6
,
X\in\underline{\mathscr{G}}
if and only if
\Phi^{\prime}(X)\in\underline{\mathscr{G}}
. This implies
\Phi^{\prime\prime}(\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}})=\Phi^{\prime}(\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}})\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{G}}
.

Next, we show that the functors in
(\sharp)
induce triangle functors among quotient categories.

By Corollary
3.13
,
\mathscr{S}
contains
{\rm Add}(M)
and is closed under taking
\Omega_{M}
and
\Omega_{M}^{-}
in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
. Since
{\rm Add}(M)\subseteq\mathscr{G}
and
\mathscr{C}
is a triangulated category by Lemma
3.17
,
(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
. Note that
\Phi({\rm Add}(\underline{M}))={\rm Add}(\underline{M})
and
\Psi({\rm Add}(\underline{M}))=0
due to
\underline{M}\in\underline{\mathscr{S}}
. By Lemma
2.1
(1), the adjoint pairs
({\rm inc},\widetilde{\Phi})
and
(\widetilde{\Psi},{\rm inc})
in
(\sharp)
induce adjoint pairs
({\rm inc},\widetilde{\Phi}_{0})
and
(\widetilde{\Psi}_{0},\pi\circ{\rm inc})
of additive functors among triangulated categories:

(\natural)\quad\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.87502pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.87502pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.44177pt\raise 5.35417pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.35417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi\circ{\rm inc}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.01823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.01823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.19402pt\raise 7.36111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\;\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.38368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.53207pt\raise 22.93463pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 8.66336pt\raise 8.99124pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 90.38368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.79817pt\raise 20.95421pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.35417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 56.23708pt\raise 5.29541pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

In this diagram, both
{\rm inc}
and
\pi\circ{\rm inc}
(see Lemma
4.2
(2)) are fully faithful triangle functors.
It is known that any left or right adjoint of a triangle functor between triangulated categories is again a triangle functor.
Thus
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}
are triangle functors.
In the following, we show that both
\pi\circ{\rm inc}
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
have right adjoints.

According to Lemma
3.12
(3),
\underline{\mathscr{T}}={\rm Coloc}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})={\rm Im}(\Phi^{\prime})
. By the definition of
\mathscr{T}
,
if
X\in\underline{\mathscr{T}}
, then
\Psi^{\prime}(X)=0
and
\Phi^{\prime}(X)\simeq X
. This means that

\Psi^{\prime}({\rm Prod}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)}))=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Phi^{\prime}({\rm Prod}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)}))={\rm Prod}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)}).

Since
{\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))={\rm add}(M)
and
{{}_{A}}M\in A\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, there holds
{\rm Prod}(\underline{\nu_{A}(M)})={\rm Prod}(\underline{M})={\rm Add}(\underline{M})
.
It follows that
\Psi^{\prime}({\rm Add}(\underline{M}))=0
and
\Phi^{\prime}({\rm Add}(\underline{M}))={\rm Add}(\underline{M})
. Since
{\rm Add}(\underline{M})\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}
and
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=\Phi^{\prime}\circ{\rm inc}
, we have
\Phi^{\prime\prime}({\rm Add}(\underline{M}))={\rm Add}(\underline{M})
.
Due to Lemma
2.1
(1),
\Phi^{\prime\prime}:\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}\to\underline{\mathscr{G}}
induces a functor
\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{0}:(\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]\to\mathscr{C}
which is a right adjoint of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
, while

\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}:\underline{\mathscr{G}}\to\underline{M}^{\bot}
induces a functor
\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}_{0}:\mathscr{C}\to\underline{M}^{\bot}
which is a right adjoint of
\pi\circ{\rm inc}
.

When acting on objects,
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\Phi}
are the same. So we denote
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
by
\widetilde{\Phi}
for simplicity.
Similarly, we denote
\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}
,
\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}_{0}
and
\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{0}
by
\widetilde{\Psi}
,
\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}
and
\Phi^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.

To show the existence of the recollement of
\mathscr{C}
in Proposition
4.3
,
it remains to show the existence of two canonical triangles in Definition
2.2
(4).
Let
X\in\mathscr{G}
. We have shown that there is a canonical triangle
{\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}(X)\to X\to{\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Psi}(X)\to{\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}(X)[1]
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
such that
\widetilde{\Phi}(X)\in\underline{\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}(X)\in\underline{M}^{\bot}
. Since
\underline{M}^{\bot}={{}^{\bot}}\underline{M}\subseteq\underline{{{}^{\bot 1}}M}
by Lemma
4.2
(1), it follows from Lemma
\ref{M-stable category}(2)
that this triangle induces a triangle
{\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}(X)\to X\to{\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Psi}(X)\to\Omega_{M}^{-}({\rm inc}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}(X))
in
\mathscr{C}
, which is the required second triangle in Definition
2.2
(4). Similarly, by the triangle
\Psi^{\prime}(X)\to X\to\Phi^{\prime}(X)\to\Psi^{\prime}(X)[1]
with
\Psi^{\prime}(X)\in\underline{M}^{\bot}
and
\Phi^{\prime}(X)\in\underline{\mathscr{G}}
and by the inclusion

\underline{M}^{\bot}\subseteq\underline{M^{\bot 1}}
, we can obtain the first triangle in Definition
2.2
(4).
Thus the proof of Proposition
4.3
is completed.

\square

Now, we define a full subcategory
\mathscr{E}
of
\mathscr{G}
, which contains both
\mathscr{G}_{0}
and
A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
.

\mathscr{E}:=\{X\in\mathscr{G}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X),\,{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M[1],X)\in\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\}.

Lemma 4.4
.

(1)

\mathscr{E}=\{X\in\mathscr{G}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X)\in\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}},\,{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M)\in\Gamma^{\rm op}\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\}
.

(2)

\mathscr{E}
is a thick subcategory of
\mathscr{G}
and
\mathscr{E}/[M]
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
.

Proof.

(1)
As
A
is an Artin algebra over an Artin commutative ring
R
and
{{}_{A}}M
is finitely generated,
\Gamma
is an Artin algebra
over
R
. Moreover, a
\Gamma
-module
N
is finitely generated if and only if
{}_{R}N
is finitely generated as an
R
-module. Thus
(1)
follows
from
D{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M[1],X)\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,\nu_{A}(M))
and
{\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))={\rm add}(M)
.

(2)
Clearly,
\mathscr{E}
is closed under direct summands in
\mathscr{G}
. Now, let
0\to X_{1}\to X_{2}\to X_{3}\to 0
be an exact sequence in
A\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
with
X_{i}\in\mathscr{G}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
. Since
\underline{\mathscr{G}}=\underline{M}^{\bot\neq 0,-1}
by Lemma
4.2
, there is a long exact sequence in
R\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
:

0\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M[1],X_{1})\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M[1],X_{2})\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M[1],X_{3})\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X_{1})\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X_{2})\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X_{3})\to 0.

This implies that
\mathscr{E}
has the two out of three property in
\mathscr{G}
. Thus
\mathscr{E}
is a thick subcategory of
\mathscr{G}
.

By definition,
\mathscr{E}/[M]
consists of all objects
X\in\mathscr{C}
which is
M
-stably isomorphic to an object of
\mathscr{E}
.
To show that
\mathscr{E}/[M]
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
, it suffices to show that
\mathscr{E}/[M]
is closed under taking
\Omega_{M}
and
\Omega_{M}^{-}
in
\mathscr{C}
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{E}
. Due to
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X)\in\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, we see that
X
has a right
{\rm Add}(M)
-approximation
f:M_{0}\oplus P_{0}\to X
such that
M_{0}\in{\rm add}(M)
and
P_{0}\in A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
. Note that
\mathscr{E}
contains
{\rm add}(M)
and
A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
. This forces
M_{0}\oplus P_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
, and further

{\rm Ker}(f)\in\mathscr{E}
. It follows from
\Omega_{M}(X)\simeq{\rm Ker}(f)
in
\mathscr{C}
that
\Omega_{M}(X)\in\mathscr{E}/[M]
. Since
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,M)
is a
\Gamma^{\rm op}
-module, we can show
\Omega_{M}^{-}(X)\in\mathscr{E}/[M]
.

The proof of
(2)
also implies that if
X\in\mathscr{G}
, then
X\in\mathscr{E}
if and only if it has a complete
{\rm Add}(M)
-resolution
M^{\bullet}_{X}:=(M_{X}^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
satisfying that
M_{X}^{i}=N^{i}\oplus P^{i}
with
N^{i}\in{\rm add}(M)
and
P^{i}\in A\mbox{{\rm-Proj}}
.
\square

Corollary 4.5
.

The recollement in Proposition
4.3
can be restricted a recollement of
\mathscr{E}/[M]
:

\textstyle{\underline{M}^{\bot}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi\circ{\rm inc}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}/[M]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Psi}}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M].\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm inc}}

Proof.
Note that
\big((\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]\big)\cap(\mathscr{E}/[M])=(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
and the image of the functor
\pi\circ{\rm inc}:\underline{M}^{\bot}\to\mathscr{C}
belongs to
\mathscr{E}/[M]
. Thus Corollary
4.5
is a consequence of Proposition
4.3
.
\square

4.2
Compacts objects and representability of homological functors

In this subsection, we find out a special compact object in
\mathscr{C}
(or even in a bigger relative stable category) and establish a series of homological functors from
\mathscr{C}
to
\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
(see Theorem
4.14
).

From now on, let
{\bf L}:A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\to\mathscr{Y}
be the left adjoint of the inclusion
\mathscr{Y}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
and let
{\bf R}:A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\to\mathscr{X}
be the right adjoint of the inclusion
\mathscr{X}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.
Define

H^{0}_{*}:=[1]\circ{\bf L}\circ[-1]\circ{\bf R}:\;\;A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{H},\;\;N:=H^{0}_{*}(M)\in\mathscr{H}\;\;\mbox{and}

\mathscr{H}^{\rm fg}:=\{X\in\mathscr{H}\mid{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X)\in\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\}.

Then
H^{0}_{*}
is a homological functor, that is, for a triangle
X_{1}\to X_{2}\to X_{3}\to X_{1}[1]
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
,
the sequence
H^{0}_{*}(X_{1})\to H^{0}_{*}(X_{2})\to H^{0}_{*}(X_{3})
is exact in
\mathscr{H}
.
Moreover,
\mathscr{H}^{\rm fg}=\mathscr{H}\cap\underline{\mathscr{E}}
by Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
(1). Note that
D{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,N[-1])\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(N,\nu_{A}(M))
by
(\lozenge)
. Since
{\rm add}(\nu_{A}(M))={\rm add}(M)
and
N\in\mathscr{H}
, we get
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(N,M)=0
.
This implies
{\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(N)={\rm End}_{\mathscr{C}}(N)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{X}
.

(1)
There is a canonical triangle

[1]\circ{\bf R}\circ[-1](X)\to X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}_{*}(X)\to[2]\circ{\bf R}\circ[-1](X)

in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
.

(2)
If
Y\in\mathscr{Y}[1]
, then
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(\tau_{X},Y):{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(H^{0}_{*}(X),Y)\to{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(X,Y)
is an isomorphism.
In particular, there is a natural isomorphism

{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(\tau_{M},-):\;\;{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(N,-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,-):\;\;\mathscr{H}\longrightarrow\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}.

Proof.
Since
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
is a torsion pair in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
, each
A
-module
W
is endowed with a triangle
{\bf R}(W)\to W\to{\bf L}(W)\to{\bf R}(W)[1]
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. We take
W=X[-1]
and get a triangle
{\bf R}(X[-1])\to X[-1]\to{\bf L}(X[-1])\to{\bf R}(X[-1])[1]
in
A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
. Shifting this triangle by
[1]
yields another triangle

({\dagger})\quad{\bf R}(X[-1])[1]\to X\to{\bf L}(X[-1])[1]\to{\bf R}(X[-1])[2].

Due to
X\in\mathscr{X}
, we have
{\bf R}(X)\simeq X
. Thus
H^{0}_{*}(X)=[1]\circ{\bf L}\circ[-1]\circ{\bf R}(X)\simeq{\bf L}(X[-1])[1].
Then the triangle
({\dagger})
can be rewritten as

[1]\circ{\bf R}\circ[-1](X)\to X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}_{*}(X)\to[2]\circ{\bf R}\circ[-1](X).
This shows
(1)
.

Since
\mathscr{X}\subseteq A\mbox{{\rm-{\lx@text@underline{Mod}}}}
is closed under
[1]
, it follows from
{\bf R}(X[-1])\in\mathscr{X}
that both
[1]\circ{\bf R}\circ[-1](X)
and
[2]\circ{\bf R}\circ[-1](X)
belong to
\mathscr{X}
. As
\underline{{\rm Hom}}_{A}(X,Y)=0
for
X\in\mathscr{X}
and
Y\in\mathscr{Y}
, the first part of
(2)
holds by
(1)
, while the second part of
(2)
follows from
M\in\mathscr{X}
and
\mathscr{H}\subseteq\mathscr{Y}[1]
.
\square

The next result follows from
[
13
, Chap. III, Lemma 3.3 and Theorem 3.4]
; see also
[
24
, Theorem 1.3(3)]
for the assertion
(3)
.

Lemma 4.7
.

(1)
For any
X\in\mathscr{X}
, the morphism
\tau_{X}
in Lemma
4.6
(1) induces an isomorphism of
\Gamma
-modules:

{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,\tau_{X}):{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,H^{0}_{*}(X)).

(2)
The functor
H^{0}_{*}
induces an isomorphism
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(N)
of algebras such that

\gamma\,\tau_{M}=\tau_{M}H^{0}_{*}(\gamma)
for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
. In this sense,
\Gamma
can be identified with
{\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(N)
.

(3)

N
is a small projective generator of
\mathscr{H}
and the functor

{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(N,-):\mathscr{H}\to\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-Mod}}
is an equivalence of abelian categories.

An easy observation is the following result, of which
(2)
conveys that
N
has only finitely many indecomposable direct summands in
\mathscr{C}
.

Corollary 4.8
.

(1)
There is a fully faithful functor
\Theta:\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\to(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
which sends
\Gamma
to
N
.

(2)
Let
M=A\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}M_{i}
, where
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
M_{i}
are indecomposable and non-projective. Then
N\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}H_{*}^{0}(M_{i})

in
\mathscr{C}
and
H_{*}^{0}(M_{i})
are indecomposable in
\mathscr{C}
.

(3)
Let
X\in\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}
. Then there is a triangle

X_{0}\to X\to H^{0}_{*}(X)\to\Omega_{M}^{-}(X_{0})
in
\mathscr{C}
such that
X_{0}\in\mathscr{G}\cap\mathscr{S}
and
\Omega_{M}(X_{0})\in\mathscr{H}
.
Further, if
X\in\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S}
, then
\Omega_{M}(X_{0}),H^{0}_{*}(X)\in\mathscr{H}^{\rm fg}
.

Proof.

(1)
By Lemma
4.2
(2)(b), the composition
U:\mathscr{H}^{\rm fg}=\mathscr{H}\cap\underline{\mathscr{E}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S}}\to(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
is fully faithful. Moreover, from Lemmas
4.6
(2) and
4.7
(3) it follows that
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,-)\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(N,-):\mathscr{H}^{\rm fg}\to\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}
, which is an equivalence of abelian categories sending
N
to
\Gamma
. In particular,
N\in\mathscr{H}^{\rm fg}
. Now, let
\Theta:\Gamma\mbox{{\rm-mod}}\to(\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{S})/[M]
be the composition of a quasi-inverse of
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,-)
with
U
. Then
\Theta
is fully faithful and sends
\Gamma
to
N
.

(2)
By Lemmas
4.6
(2) and
4.7
(1),
H_{*}^{0}
induces an isomorphism
{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(M,M_{i})\simeq{\rm\underline{Hom}}_{A}(N,H_{*}^{0}(M_{i}))
.
Thus
{\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(M_{i})\simeq{\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(H_{*}^{0}(M_{i}))
as algebras. It then follows from
H_{*}^{0}(M_{i})\in\mathscr{H}
that
{\rm\underline{End}}_{A}(H_{*}^{0}(M_{i}))\simeq{\rm End}_{\mathscr{C}}(H_{*}^{0}(M_{i}))
as algebras by Lemma
4.2
(2).
As
M_{i}
